# 1.6.1e — The fuzz findings F-018…F-028 (DEF-144…DEF-154): the silent wrong answers, the destroyed arena, the compiler's own stops, the two tables

**Status: PLANNED execution-grade 2026-09-26 (`nitpick-compiler_19`, while landings 73–76's
harnesses ran); STEP 1 LANDED 2026-09-26 (`nitpick-compiler_20`; the execution record at the end:
DEF-144…DEF-148 fixed, DEF-157 and DEF-158 found by its probes and fixed, DEF-159 registered; S-115 ratified
as D-334); FOUR landings, each one commit under the in-process checks and a full harness,
`parity` green, the manifest gate's two zeros quoted (`npkg verify --record` in every commit,
D-040). Step 1 lands BEFORE 1.6.1d's remaining steps 3b and 4 — the user, 2026-09-26: "the
order you proposed is fine with me" — by the standing rule that a program the compiler accepts
and answers wrongly, or a memory fault in safe code, outranks a leak and an over-restriction.
Then 1.6.1d 3b and 4, then steps 2–4 of this subcycle, then 1.6.1 step 2.** One question is
the user's before step 1's DEF-146 lands (S-115, §3); every other decision is taken here.

The evidence is the library listener's M11 (`nitpick-libs/nitpick-fuzz/findings/F-018…F-028`,
main `3d7d924`; `m11/RESULTS.md`, `REPORT.md` §12): the references checked against the
compiler, 3,704 of 10,433 reference lines covered, 1,488 claims, 1,262 tested at `9126350`,
144 disagreeing — the same way at `c3bdae2` and `1b4f0c6`. **Reproduced here first, on step
3's compiler (`3235308`), by the fuzzer's recipe at both legs (`.internal/repro.sh`)**: every
one of the 26 programs of F-018…F-024 and F-026 answers exactly as the listener's VERDICTS
say (h1/h2 10, u1 10, u2 TYPE-007, j1 10, t1 10, a1 96, i1 `llc!`/`opt!`, c1/c2/c3 npkc 3,
d1 10; every control 0 or its named refusal), and F-025's canary is refused under the flag.

## 0. What 1.6.1e is, and is not

Eleven findings in four classes. **Silent wrong answers** — a macro's free name reading the
call site's local (DEF-144), a `\u{…}` escape typed `char8` and truncated (DEF-145), the
scope-exit join relaying the last-spawned child's error (DEF-146), an expired `timedwait`
returning success (DEF-147). **A memory fault in safe code** — a `shared_arena` destroyed
while a spawned thread holds it (DEF-148). **The compiler's own refusals and stops** — an
un-awaited async METHOD call accepted and emitted as an undefined symbol (DEF-149), three
traps of npkc itself (DEF-150), `--extra-picky=no-wildx` refusing every program at its own
prelude (DEF-151), a unit annotation on `tfp64` accepted and ignored (DEF-152). **The two
tables** — sixteen rows where the compiler departs from the reference safely (DEF-153) and
ninety-four rows where the reference is wrong or stale (DEF-154).

It is NOT: a change to any rule the listener did not find wrong (every fix restores what a
decision or a reference already says, and one question — which child's error a join relays —
is put to the user because no text decides it); a snapshot refresh (nothing in `src/` needs
the new emitter; measured at each step by the emission comparison); a floor byte (DEF-147's
fix is in the emitted wait, not the floor).

## 1. Where it starts (main's chain at `3235308`; every line number is that tree's)

**DEF-144.** `resolve_expr` (resolve.npk 167–185) switches to `module_scope` under a
`HYG_ROOT` node and a statement macro's block takes the module as its parent (318–340): the
RESOLVER binds a body's free name to the module binding correctly, and records the symbol on
the node (`ast_set_expr_a`, 111). The EMITTER does not read it: `emit_ident` (ir_expr.npk
1651) asks `fnem_lookup_idx(x.fe, name)` — the function's local table, BY NAME, innermost
first — and consults the node's symbol only when no local carries the name. A caller's local
`lvl` therefore wins over the module's `fixed int32:lvl` the resolver chose. Arithmetic reads
right by accident: `lvl + 0i32` is folded by the constant folder, which reads the resolver's
symbol. The same name-first lookup stands in `addr_of_place`'s identifier case, in
`clear_root_flag` (ir_stmt.npk 804) and wherever `fnem_lookup_idx(x.fe, <name>)` resolves an
identifier NODE (nine sites; the parameter's flag lookup in `emit_assign` is by name too).

**DEF-145.** The lexer decodes an escape or a source character to a code point (`cp`,
lexer.npk 421–457) and makes one token kind, `CharLit` (token_kind.npk 21, "payload: the code
point"); the parser stores the payload with no width (parse_expr.npk 641–644); the typer types
EVERY `ExprCharLiteral` as `char8` (type_expr.npk 3584: `tt_char(e.types, 8i32)`); the folder
does the same (type_resolve.npk 1199); the emitter writes `i8 <payload>` (ir_expr.npk 719),
which LLVM truncates. `'\u{1F641}'` is 128577; its low byte is 65.

**DEF-146.** `emit_scope_join` (ir_stmt.npk 100–336) walks `join_head`'s chain — a LIFO, the
last-spawned child at the head — and at `lgot` (261–300) stores a child's error into the
result slot unless the slot ALREADY holds one (`jown`, 288: `icmp ne i32 jce, 0` → `lhead`).
The first error the walk meets is the newest child's; D-207's "the function's own error wins"
and "the first child error wins" read one slot, so the second is decided by walk order. No
text defines "first": D-163's exception paragraph (DECISIONS 11493) and CONCURRENCY_REFERENCE
§2.2:79 say "the first child error, verbatim (D-080), after every child has finished".

**DEF-147.** `emit_condvar_wait` (ir_expr.npk 6046–6300): phase 0 links the frame on the
condvar and parks under the absolute deadline (`npk_cv_begin`); on resume, phase 1 (`reacq`,
6198) re-acquires the mutex through `npk_mutex_acquire_wait`, which answers 0 (taken), 1
(parked, retry when woken) or −4107 (the deadline passed while waiting for the mutex —
npkrt.ll 1520–1545); `eslot` takes the answer; `fin` builds the `Result` from it and nulls
the guard when it is not 0. NOTHING RECORDS THAT PHASE 0 ENDED BY THE DEADLINE: a wait no one
signals resumes at the deadline, re-acquires an uncontended mutex at once (0), and returns
success after the full wait. The wind-up path (`wu`, `wu2`) already knows how to spend the
guard mid-wait: it nulls the guard's slot, steps off the list and fails with −4107.

**DEF-148.** `escape_spawn_args` (escape.npk 4325–4385) admits a `shared_arena<T>->` (and the
four locks' pointers) across a spawn under D-180, on the argument that a scope's JOIN
precedes its drop (1.4.4's order). The aliasing walk (alias.npk) knows no spawn: the call's
parties end with the call (`acc_call`, 1451; `list_truncate(@x.live, mark)` at its end), so
after `drop filler(@s)` nothing stands between the owner and `s.destroy()` (type_members.npk
3778; the move analysis merely invalidates `s`, bindings.npk 1389). The thread then allocates
in a destroyed arena and `exit 0` traps `WildLeak`. CONCURRENCY_REFERENCE §5.2:321: "`destroy`
on a shared arena requires that no thread still holds handles … the owner destroys it after
joining."

**DEF-149.** The free-call path refuses a bare call of an `async` function (type_members.npk
833–842: `decl_has(d, DECL_ASYNC)` and `id.index != e.await_operand` → TYPE-043; the spawn
form sets `await_operand` around its operand, type_expr.npk 3781). `type_method_call` (1967)
resolves the method (`find_method`, 2157; the UFCS lookup after it) and never asks the
declaration whether it is `async`; the channel, mutex, rwlock, condvar and barrier method
paths each carry their own copy of the await rule (3192, 3271, 3315, 3375, 3427), a trait's or
an impl's async method has none. The emitter then names the plain symbol of a function that
exists only as a coroutine.

**DEF-150.** (c1, c3) `expand_splice` (expand.npk) → `record_site` (its `MACRO_BODY_DECLS`
arm, `ast_decl(DeclId{ index: idx })`) raises `ast.BoundsId` (the backtraces: `npk_raise` ←
`ast_decl` ← `record_site` ← `expand_splice` ← `expand_impl_window` / `expand_module_here`);
the parser marks a body `DECLS` the moment one item is a declaration and keeps STATEMENT
items beside it in the same window (parse_decl.npk 1355–1372: `has_decl` chooses per item),
so a mixed body's statement ids are read as declaration ids — the exact shape is settled at
step 2's first act by dumping `kind` and `idx` at the raise. (c2) `fold_expr`/`fold_binary`
(type_resolve.npk 1176, 1330) recurse per nesting level with a NODE-count fuel
(`FOLD_FUEL` 4096, line 761), not a depth bound; every analysis walk bounds its depth at 256
and refuses by name (ASSIGN-003, BORROW-007, LOCK-004, PICK-005 at 260 deep, measured), so a
500-deep expression traps the folder's recursion through the split-stack prologue
(`npk_stack_exhausted` ← `__morestack` ← `fold_binary`) before any analysis can refuse it,
and npkc's own `failsafe` maps every trap to exit 3 with no message. MACRO_REFERENCE §5:250
promises a depth bound reported as a compile error.

**DEF-151.** `reject_wildx` (pipeline.npk 97–125) tests `decl_flags(d) & DECL_RET_WILDX` on
EVERY declaration kind; `decl_flags` (parse_decl.npk 112) answers `d.a` for all but two
kinds, and for a kind whose `a` is a type or a window (an impl, a field, a variant, a
parameter …) the bit is the parity of some node index — DEF-93's lesson, one kind over. The
prelude holds no `wildx` (0 occurrences) and produced 256 of them. The check also sees only
two of the four spellings: a `wildx` local and a `wildx`-returning function; an `=>! wildx`
cast (`expr_cast_quals & QUAL_WILDX`) and a call of the four `wildx_*` builtins (`builtins.npk`
71–74) escape it. And it runs from `npkc.npk` (169) alone: `tools/check.npk` — the checker
the rejection suites drive — knows no flag, which is why the harness excuses WILDX-003 from
`check_codes_tested` as "build-mode -- only under --extra-picky=no-wildx" (harness.py 636).

**DEF-152.** `resolve_type`'s builtin path (type_resolve.npk 2360–2700) reads the type
arguments for the parameterised builtins (`simd`, `complex`, `dim256`, …) and, for every
other builtin kind, falls to the plain interning (2688 for `TY_TFP`: `tt_tfp(r.types,
spec.bits)`) with the arguments never counted — `tfp64<Meters>` is `tfp64`.

## 2. The design

### 2.1 DEF-144 — the emitter reads the resolver's binding (step 1)

**The fix.** ONE resolver of an identifier node to its storage, `ident_slot(x, id, n)` in
ir_expr.npk, used by `emit_ident`, `addr_of_place`'s identifier case, `clear_root_flag` and
every other site that resolves an IDENTIFIER NODE through `fnem_lookup_idx` (enumerated at
the step's first act by grep; the parameter-flag lookup in `emit_assign` included). The rule,
by the symbol the resolver recorded (`n.a`): a `SYM_STMT` symbol → the slot declared by that
statement (`fnem_lookup_decl(x.fe, s.origin)`, the by-declaration lookup D-286 added), falling
back to the name only when no slot is registered under the statement; a `SYM_DECL` symbol
whose declaration is a `DeclParamDecl` → the parameter's slot by name (parameters are bound
by name in the prologue and are never shadowed by an expansion's names, which live in a block
of their own); every other `SYM_DECL` (a function, an error constant, a global) →
`emit_decl_value`, before any local is consulted; a `SYM_PAT` symbol → the pattern binding's
slot by name (bound at the arm; a body's block never redeclares it); no symbol (`n.a == 0`: a
bare builtin's name) → the name lookup as today. So a macro body's `lvl` reaches the module's
binding whatever the caller declared, and a caller's `f` of `int32` type no longer shadows
the module function `f()` the body calls (the same class in `emit_call`'s function-value
lookup, DEF-54's corner: a callee identifier whose symbol is a function declaration is a
declared function, whatever local shares its name). The folder's accidental correctness
(`lvl + 0i32` = 7) is not relied on; it is stated in the record.

**Tests.** `tests/backend/programs/macro_hygiene_names.npk`: the listener's four shapes (the
lone identifier, the comparison operand in a statement macro, the two arithmetic controls),
a body calling a module function whose name the caller holds as a local, a body reading a
module binding under a caller local of a DIFFERENT type (an emitter that read the caller's
slot loaded the wrong width), `#caller(lvl)` beside the bare name in one body (the two
differ), and the same shapes inside an `async` function (frame slots) — each its own exit,
both legs. `tests/backend/ir_expr.npk`'s expectations may move by the lookup's text: run
`harness.py --only tests/backend/` before the commit.

### 2.2 DEF-145 — a character literal has a width (step 1)

**The rule** (TYPE_REFERENCE §2.3:280, `ty0280`): a `'\u{…}'` escape is a `char32` literal,
whatever its value, and is refused in a `char8` or `char16` slot as any mismatch is
(TYPE-007); a plain character or a `\x` escape is a `char8` literal as today; a SOURCE
character whose code point exceeds 0xFF (`'😀'`) is a `char32` literal too — the one gap the
reference leaves, decided the safe way here (a code point that does not fit a byte was never
a `char8`; truncating it in silence is DEF-145's own defect) and stated in the reference.
`'é'` (233) stays `char8`, as it has since 1.0.8.

**The fix.** The lexer makes two token kinds, `CharLit` (8) and `CharLit32` (32) — the
escape form or a code point above 0xFF chooses the second; the parser writes the width into
the node's `a` (8 or 32; `ast_add_expr(…, ExprCharLiteral, sp, width, 0, 0, cp)`); the typer,
the folder (`cv_char(tt_char(r.types, width), payload)`), `pattern_const` (ir_stmt.npk 2150:
a `char32` selector's pattern emits the full code point under the selector's width, as it
does today by reading the payload) and the emitter (`i8`/`i32` by the node's type) read the
width; the encoder's `TY_CHAR` terms already carry the type's bits. A `char16` literal has no
spelling (the reference offers none); a numeric with the suffix (`128512char32`) is the other
spelling and stays.

**Tests.** `tests/backend/programs/char_literal_width.npk`: `char32:c = '\u{1F600}'` (0),
`'\u{E9}'` in a `char32` (233), a `char32` `pick` with `\u{}` arms, `'é'` in a `char8` (233),
`'\x41'` (65), the `char32` compare with the numeric spelling; `tests/types/rejection/
char_escape_width.npk`: `'\u{1F641}'` and `'\u{41}'` in a `char8` slot, a `char16` slot —
exactly {TYPE-007}, three sites; the listener's u1 becomes a refusal and u2 runs 0.

### 2.3 DEF-146 — the join relays the FIRST-SPAWNED child's error; S-115 (step 1)

**The question — S-115 (§3).** "First" is undefined. Two readings: the first child error in
SPAWN ORDER (program text; deterministic under every schedule), or the first to OCCUR in time
(needs a stamp the floor does not keep; under the explorer's schedules two runs of one program
would relay different errors). **Recommended: spawn order** — the join relays the error of
the earliest-spawned child that failed. The listener's `j1` expects E1 under either reading.

**The fix under the recommendation.** The walk is LIFO, so "the earliest-spawned failing
child" is the LAST error the walk meets: a child's error OVERWRITES one a previous child of
this walk stored, and never the function's own. One hoisted `i8` alloca per join, `jtook`
(0 before the loop): at `lgot`, with `jerr2 != 0` — if the result slot holds an error and
`jtook` is 0 (the function's own, stored at the seam before the unwind, D-207) → `lhead`
(the body's error wins, as today); else store the child's error, `jtook = 1`, `lhead`. The
join loop pumps the executor synchronously (`npk_run_until`) and never suspends this frame,
so an alloca is right in a coroutine too. `x.fe.bare` (a bare function's join) keeps its
trap path.

**Tests.** `tests/backend/programs/join_first_spawned.npk`: the listener's three (fast then
slow → E1; a single child; slow then fast → E1 too, now by rule), a three-child shape (E1
fails, ok, E3 fails → E1), the body's own error over children's (the body fails E9 after two
failing spawns → E9), thread children (the same through `thread`). Explored: `// stress: 40`
and `// explore: 1000` (the join is a scheduling point).

### 2.4 DEF-147 — an expired `timedwait` is `DeadlineExceeded`, the guard spent (step 1)

**The fix**, in `emit_condvar_wait`: at `reacq`, before `npk_mutex_acquire_wait`, `now =
mono_now(); expired = now >= deadline`; when expired the wait took the deadline path of
phase 0 — `eslot = -4107`, branch to `fin` (which steps off the condvar's list and nulls the
lent guard's slot, as the wind-up path does: the mutex is NOT held, and D-056's amendment
says the guard is SPENT) — no re-acquire. The re-acquire's own −4107 already reaches `fin`
through `eslot`; 1 (parked) keeps its park-and-retry. A signal that lands exactly at the
deadline is late by the deadline's rule. `ctl_t2` (a `recv` deadline) is unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/backend/programs/condvar_expiry.npk`: no signaller, 200 ms → the error
path with the guard spent (a re-acquire by the caller succeeds after; the mutex was not
held); a signal before the deadline → success with the guard whole; a broadcast; the
listener's `t1` as written. `// stress: 40`; `// explore: 1000` where the shim's virtual
clock lets the deadline pass. `sync_prims.npk`'s kept seed and `trap_one_failsafe`'s are
re-run first: an IR change on the wait moves every explored schedule that reaches it
(DEF-67's rule).

### 2.5 DEF-148 — a spawn LENDS its sanctioned crossing until the scope's join (step 1)

**The rule.** A `drop f(…)` (or `thread` spawn) that hands a task a `shared_arena<T>->` or a
lock's pointer (D-180's five crossings) LENDS the root binding to the task until the enclosing
block's exit — the join (D-207). Until then the owner may do what the type's concurrency
contract allows — a shared arena's `alloc` and `get` (D-154: a slot is written once, before
its handle escapes), a lock's `acquire`/`read`/`write`/`timedwait`/`signal`/`broadcast`/
`arrive` — and may hand the same borrow to another spawn; it may NOT end the value's life or
identity: `destroy()`, an assignment over the root, a `move` out of it, `$$m`, or `@root` to
a callee whose summary writes what the type's contract does not (a callee that `destroy`s).
Refused as `NITPICK-BORROW-016` at the access, naming the spawn and the block.

**The fix**, in alias.npk: a fifth party kind, `PARTY_LENT` — pushed AFTER `acc_call`'s own
truncation for each such argument of a spawn (the walk learns the spawn as the escape
analysis does: a `drop` of a call whose callee is `async`, or a `thread` call; `callee_is_async`
becomes `pub` in escape.npk), rooted at the argument's place root, holder 0, live until the
enclosing block's `frame_pop`. `conflicts(PARTY_LENT, ACC_WRITE|ACC_EXCLUSIVE)` is true;
`check_access_reported` skips a LENT party when the access is the RECEIVER of a method call
in the type's concurrent set (`acc_call` computes `lent_ok` from the receiver's kind and the
method's name and passes it down), and `acc_call` skips the LENT parties for an argument of
another SPAWN. The escape analysis's mutation summary stops recording a shared arena's
`alloc`/`get` and a lock's concurrent operations as writes of the pointee
(`record_mut_of_place`'s receiver case: the kinds' own sets), so `admit_written(@s)` to a
helper that only allocates is admitted and a helper that destroys is refused. Message: "`s`
is lent to the task spawned at L:C until the end of this block, whose exit joins it (D-180,
D-207): `destroy`, an assignment, a `move` or `$$m` before the join would end a value the task
still holds — destroy it after the block, or join first".

**Tests.** `tests/analysis/rejection/spawn_lent.npk`: `s.destroy()` after a spawn holding
`@s` (BORROW-016), `s = other` (BORROW-016), `move(s)` (BORROW-016), `$$m s` (BORROW-016),
`@s` to a callee that destroys (BORROW-016), a `Mutex` moved while a spawn holds it
(BORROW-016); the controls silent: `s.alloc`/`s.get` by the owner while lent, `@s` to a
second spawn, `@s` to a helper that only allocates, `destroy` after the block, the
listener's a2 and a3. `tests/backend/programs/spawn_lent_ok.npk`: the owner and two tasks
allocating in one arena, destroyed after the join (exit 0 both legs, `// stress: 40`,
`// explore: 1000`).

### 2.6 DEF-149 — an un-awaited async METHOD call is refused (step 2)

**The fix.** In `type_method_call`, once the callee declaration is final (the impl method,
the trait default, or the UFCS free function — the one point that reads `did_index` to
compute the call's type), the free path's rule verbatim: `decl_has(d, DECL_ASYNC)` and
`id.index != e.await_operand` → TYPE-043 ("an `async` method is called under `await` or
spawned with `drop` (D-058/D-177)"); the spawn form `drop r.run()` sets `await_operand` and
stays. The five builtin method paths keep their copies (each names its operation).

**Tests.** `tests/types/rejection/async_method_bare.npk`: the listener's `r.read(buf, d)`
bound to a `Result`, a bare `obj.run()` on an impl's async method, a bare call through a
`dyn` — exactly {TYPE-043}; controls: awaited, spawned with `drop`.

### 2.7 DEF-150 — the compiler's three stops (step 2)

- **c1, c3 — a mixed macro body.** The first act dumps `macro_body_kind` and each item's id
  at the raise; the fix is the parser's classification (`p_parse_macro`, parse_decl.npk
  1346–1372): a body that mixes declarations and statements is refused at the declaration by
  name — `NITPICK-MACRO-0NN` (the next free code), "a macro body is declarations or statements,
  not both (MACRO_REFERENCE §3)" — because no site accepts both kinds (a struct body takes
  statements, every other site declarations), and `record_site` reads every item by the
  body's one kind. If the dump shows a PURE declaration body reaching the raise (a method
  parsed as a statement, say), the parser's item classification is the defect and the fix
  is there; the reference's two examples (`emit_methods` into an impl, a `comptime` function
  emitted and folded) must run. `tests/backend/programs/macro_emit_decls.npk` (both
  examples), `tests/expansion/rejection/macro_mixed_body.npk`.
- **c2 — the depth bound is the parser's.** `EXPR_DEPTH_MAX = 256` in parse_expr.npk (the
  analyses' `ALIAS_DEPTH`/`BORROW_FUEL` family is 256, and 260 deep refuses by their names
  today; the folder's recursion exhausts the compiler's 8 MiB stack between 450 and 500):
  the expression parser counts its nesting and refuses the 257th level with
  `NITPICK-PARSE-0NN` at the node ("this expression nests deeper than 256 levels; split it
  — no walk of the compiler follows further, MACRO_REFERENCE §5"). An EXPANSION that clones
  deeper than the bound is caught by the same rule applied by the expander after each
  instantiation (`CLONE_FUEL`'s exhaustion becomes a diagnostic if it is silent today —
  measured at the step). The folder's `FOLD_FUEL` stays as a belt.
  `tests/frontend/parse_depth.npk` (256 parses, 257 refuses) and the listener's c2/c4.

### 2.8 DEF-151 — `--extra-picky=no-wildx` reads the four spellings, of the program, in the checker (step 2)

**The fix.** `reject_wildx` moves into the front half as a MODE of `front_run` (a field of
`Front`, set by the driver and by `tools/check.npk` from the same `--extra-picky=no-wildx`
argument), runs over the PROGRAM's modules (never the prelude's), and reads: a `wildx` local
(`StmtVarDeclStmt` quals), a `wildx`-returning FUNCTION (`DeclFunctionDecl` and the extern
method, the kinds whose flags live in `a`), an `=>! wildx …->` cast (`expr_cast_quals`), and a
call of `wildx_alloc`/`wildx_seal`/`wildx_call`/`wildx_free` (the bare and `#` forms, by
name from the builtin table). Both runners read `// npkc-flags: <flags>` from a rejection
file's header and pass them to the tool (`read_expectations` / `expect.npk`; the tool's
argv), so the mode has a suite: `tests/analysis/rejection/no_wildx.npk` with the four
spellings (exactly {WILDX-003}, four sites) and `tests/accept/no_wildx_clean.npk` (the
canary's shape under the flag, silent); harness.py's "build-mode" excuse for WILDX-003
retires with it. The listener's `n1_canary` compiles under the flag.

### 2.9 DEF-152 — a builtin type refuses arguments it does not take (step 2)

**The fix.** After the parameterised builtins in `resolve_type`'s builtin path, any builtin
kind reached with `type_args_count(n) > 0` is `NITPICK-TYPE-020`-class `TYPE_GENERIC_ARITY`:
"`tfp64` takes no type arguments; a unit rides `dim256<Unit>` alone (D-196, TYPE_REFERENCE
§5a)". `tests/types/rejection/builtin_no_args.npk` (`tfp64<Meters>`, `int32<X>`,
`string<char8>`? — no: `string<char16>` is a spelled form in TYPE §3.2, kept — three
sites), the listener's d1 refused, d2 unchanged.

### 2.10 DEF-153 — the sixteen rows, decided (step 3)

Each row of `F-027/ROWS.md`, its decision, and where it lands:
- **a. accepted though refused** — `flt256` (`ty0176`): the keyword stays lexed and the
  builtin table refuses it by name at resolution (`TYPE_UNKNOWN`: "`flt256` is not a type;
  the float widths are 32, 64 and 128 — D-231") — a hole the checker names instead of the
  emitter; a `fixed` spliced into a struct body (`mc0085b`): refused at the splice
  (`MACRO_WRONG_SHAPE`, "a field has neither") instead of stripped; a channel's LEVEL against
  a held guard (`cc0380`) and a `dyn` method with no declared level acquiring (`vf0831`):
  the lock-ordering analysis (locks.npk) treats a `send`/`recv` on `Channel<T, LEVEL, CAP>`
  as an acquisition at LEVEL, and refuses an impl method acquiring where its trait's method
  declares no `acquires` level (LOCK-0NN each, the reference's own rules); a self-invoking
  macro declaration (`mc0246`): refused at the declaration (`MACRO-0NN`: "a macro whose body
  invokes itself never settles; MACRO_REFERENCE §5:246 marks it refused"); the `f512` and
  `flt32` suffixes (`ty0178`, `ty0189b`): the lexer's suffix production is `f32 | f64 |
  f128` (LEXICAL:315) — `LEX-0NN` at the literal.
- **b. the compiler's named holes** — `suspend_until` in a sync function (`bi0256`):
  refused by the checker (`TYPE-0NN`: "legal only inside an `async` function", BUILTIN:256)
  where it was EMIT-002; a macro invocation inside a `while`'s `decreases` measure
  (`mc0278`): the expansion walk visits the measure and invariant expressions of every loop
  form, and MACRO-006's "hole" sentence retires.
- **c. refused though permitted** — a string literal in `cstring` position (`ty0285`,
  `ty0290`, `ty0414`): LANDS WITH 1.6.1d STEP 3b (the literal is a `cstring` at `cap == 0`,
  its interior NUL a compile-time refusal, D-049 and D-328 together); an alias of a
  declaration macro at module level (`mc0046b`): the alias expands to its target's
  declarations (expand.npk's alias form gains the declaration case, D-125's "is whatever `b`
  is"); `comptime` string comparison (`mc0309b`): the folder's `fold_string_builtin` gains
  `cmp` over two folded strings (the evaluator table of MACRO:309 promises it).
- **d. two diagnostics** — MACRO-004 names both macros of the round-bound chain and points
  at the invocation, not at `prelude.npk:17:1` (`mc0251`); a `comptime` failure names the
  call chain that reached the division (`mc0344`: the evaluator carries the chain as the
  origin chain does).
Each with a case in the suite its code names; the reference sentences corrected where the
row's decision is the sentence's.

### 2.10b Step 3 as READ on `5fbaf4a` (`nitpick-compiler_22`, 2026-09-30) — this section governs where it differs from §2.10

**Measured first.** All thirteen remaining rows reproduce on `5fbaf4a` as `F-027/ROWS.md` says (the three
`cstring`-literal rows landed with 1.6.1d step 3b): `ty0176` and `bi0256` EMIT-002, `mc0278` MACRO-006,
`mc0046b` MACRO-005, `mc0309b` TYPE-004, the two scripts exit 3, the other six compile and exit 0. Each row
was then read against the compiler and probed around; the probes changed five of §2.10's decisions and found
eight defects of the rows' own neighbourhoods, registered as DEF-166 … DEF-173.

**What changed against §2.10, and why.**
- **`mc0085b` is a DOCUMENTATION row; the planned refusal is withdrawn.** A field carries qualifiers, and the
  splice has carried them across since 1.0.8 (`splice_field`: "THE QUALIFIER TRAVELS"): a `fixed int32:a;`
  spliced into a struct body IS a `fixed` field (measured: a later `s.a = 5i32;` is ASSIGN-002, exactly as on
  a field written by hand). Nothing is stripped, so nothing is lost; the reference's sentence ("a qualifier is
  refused rather than stripped: a field has neither") describes the splice before 1.0.8. The sentence is
  corrected and a test pins the travelling qualifier. Refusing would have broken D-287's `fixed` fields and
  1.0.8's own fix for the sake of a stale sentence.
- **The two suffix rows are one lexer defect, wider than the rows (DEF-166).** A float literal's text is
  scanned as `digits . ident-part*`, and everything after the numeric body that is not a known suffix is
  IGNORED: `1.5garbage`, `2.5e3zz` and `1.0f64x` compile today, `1.5f512` and `3.14flt32` among them.
- **`mc0278` is one of eight positions the expansion walk never reaches (DEF-170).** Measured: a `Rules`
  clause, `requires`, `ensures`, a function's `decreases`, a loop's `invariant` and `decreases`, an array
  size in a type, and a pick pattern's literal are each MACRO-006. One fix, by the clone's own child list.
- **`mc0046b` is refused at all three declaration sites (DEF-171)**, the struct and the impl/trait site too.
- **`bi0256` is one of four faces (DEF-168, DEF-169).** `suspend_io` in a sync body is the same EMIT-002;
  `io_watch` in a sync body COMPILES and, called where no task runs, faults the machine (`MachineFault`, exit
  107, in safe code); a park inside a `defer` body TRAPS THE COMPILER (exit 3, no message).
- **`mc0246` and `mc0251` are one fix**: a static cycle check over the macro invocation graph, at collection.
- **`mc0309b` (comptime string ordering) is NOT in this landing**: it needs the evaluator to hold an enum
  value and to run a `pick`; put to the user as its own decision (S-118) and its own landing -- SETTLED the same
  day as D-338: implemented, as step 3b.

**The rules, by work package.** Codes are existing ones unless stated; every message names its decision.

**WP-A — literals, types, the task builtins, the comptime chain** (`lexer.npk`, `numeric.npk`,
`diag_codes.npk`, `type_resolve.npk`, `type_expr.npk`, `type_members.npk`):
- **A1 (`ty0176`).** `flt256` and `flt512` are reserved words, not types (D-143; TYPE_REFERENCE §2.3). In
  `resolve_named`, ahead of `builtin_type_spec`: `NITPICK-TYPE-001` at the type, "`flt256` is a reserved word,
  not a type: the float types are `flt32`, `flt64` and `flt128` (D-143)". In every position a type stands
  (a binding, a parameter, a field, a cast target, a generic argument, a pointee). The keyword stays lexed;
  `builtin_types.npk` is generated and is not edited. One report per spelling (D-240): no second sentence
  from the cast or the initialiser.
- **A2 (`ty0178`, `ty0189b`; DEF-166).** A float literal's tail is empty or one `TypeSuffix` of
  LEXICAL_REFERENCE §3's production — nothing else. In the lexer's float arm, after the scan: split the text
  into the numeric body (`digits`, `.`, `digits`, an optional exponent `e`/`E`, optional sign, `digits`; `_`
  separators as the integer scan admits them) and the tail; a tail that is non-empty and for which
  `num_width_of` answers `WNone` is a NEW code `NITPICK-LEX-009` (`LEX_BAD_SUFFIX`), "`f512` is not a literal
  suffix: a float literal ends in `f32`, `f64`, a `tfp` width or `dim256`, or in nothing (LEXICAL_REFERENCE
  §3)", at the literal. The token returned is still a `FloatLit` (width `WNone`), NOT an `Error` token, so
  the parser adds no second sentence (D-240); the driver already stops before expansion on any diagnostic.
  Check what an exponent without a fraction digit and a doubled `e` do, and keep `0...4` a range.
- **A3 (DEF-167).** A literal WITH A FRACTION carrying an integer, `tbb` or `char` suffix (`2.5i32`) is typed
  as the integer by the checker today and dies as EMIT-002. `NITPICK-TYPE-031` at the literal, whatever the
  value (`2.0i32` too — a literal with a fraction is not an integer literal): "a literal with a fraction is a
  float or fixed-point literal; `i32` is an integer suffix, and the number in the program would not be the
  number written (D-148)". Find where a `FloatLit`'s suffix decides its type (`type_expr.npk`).
- **A4 (`bi0256`; DEF-168, DEF-169).** The three builtins that speak for the CURRENT TASK —
  `suspend_until`, `suspend_io`, `io_watch` — are legal only inside an `async` function:
  `NITPICK-TYPE-043` at the call otherwise, "`suspend_until` parks the current task and is legal only inside
  an `async` function (BUILTIN_REFERENCE §2): a synchronous function has no frame to suspend" (for
  `io_watch`: "registers the current task … a synchronous function is not one"). The two PARKS
  (`suspend_until`, `suspend_io`) are also refused inside a `defer` body, as `await` is there (same code,
  the `await` rule's own sentence about cleanup being synchronous). `io_unwatch` is unchanged: it is safe
  with no task (measured: exit 0 in a sync `main`) and a `defer` is where it belongs. The checker's
  `e.fn_is_async` and `e.in_defer` are the two facts; the site is where a bare builtin call is typed.
  After the fix `bi0256`, `p3a`, `p3d` are TYPE-043, `p3f` (the fault) is TYPE-043, `p3c` (the compiler
  trap) is TYPE-043 — and say in the report what trapped in `p3c` if it is quick to see (gdb, `break
  npk_trap`).
- **A5 (`mc0344`).** A compile-time failure names the call chain. The resolver keeps the names of the
  `comptime func:` calls being evaluated (pushed and popped in `fold_call` around the body), and
  `fold_reported` appends, when the chain is not empty, "; reached through the compile-time calls
  `outer_call` → `inner_div`" (outermost first). The span stays the offending expression's. The script's
  three checks (both names, the division's line) must pass. Use `->` if `→` is not what other messages use.

**WP-B — the lock levels** (`src/frontend/analysis/locks.npk` only):
- **B1 (`cc0380`).** A channel blocks, so `send` and `recv` on a `Channel<T, LEVEL, CAP>` are WAITS at the
  channel's LEVEL (D-056, CONCURRENCY_REFERENCE §6.1): ordered against what is held, raising no hold —
  exactly `timedwait`'s and `arrive`'s shape, so the arm goes in `sync_wait_level` (`TY_CHANNEL`; the level is
  the type's second word — verify against `tt_channel`). `close` does not block and has no level. The
  summary widens (`table_widen`) as for the other waits, so a function that sends is an acquirer at that level
  to its callers. LOCK-001 with the existing sentence, the verb named.
- **B2 (`vf0831`).** AN UNDECLARED DYNAMICALLY DISPATCHED METHOD MAY NOT ACQUIRE AT ALL (D-056, D-113 — and
  `inherit_trait_bound`'s own comment, which the code under it does not keep: with no clause on the trait's
  method it sets nothing, so the impl is unbounded and a call through `dyn` or a bound is assumed to acquire
  nothing while its impl may). A trait method with NO `acquires` clause gives every implementation of it —
  and its own default body, if it has one — a BOUND OF NOTHING: `has_bound` with a sentinel that
  `check_against_bound` reads as "may reach no level at all" (LOCK-002: "this implements a trait method
  that declares no `acquires <= N`, so a call through the trait may hold anything and this may acquire
  nothing; it can reach level K (D-056, D-113)") and that `locks_call` and `init_acquires` read as ACQUIRES
  NOTHING (never as a level: today's `lo = bound` would report "can acquire level -2147483647").
  A trait method with an EXACT clause (`acquires N`) bounds its implementations at N as the `<=` form does.
  An inherent impl (no trait) stays unbounded: nothing dispatches to it dynamically.
- The fixpoint and the silent/reporting split stay as they are. Read the whole file first (935 lines).

**WP-C — macros** (`src/frontend/macro/expand.npk`, `parse_decl.npk`, `src/driver/pipeline.npk`):
- **C1 (`mc0278`; DEF-170).** The expansion walk reaches every expression a statement or a declaration
  carries. Add, guided by what `clone_stmt`, `clone_verify`, `clone_decl` and `ast_depth.npk` already
  enumerate (so the walk and the clone agree): a loop's `invariant` and `decreases` (`while`, `when`, `for`,
  `loop`, `till`), a function's contract window (`requires`, `ensures`, `decreases`, `acquires`, `joins
  within`), a `Rules` declaration's clauses, the expressions inside TYPES (an array size, a generic VALUE
  argument: `int32[#n()]`, `Mutex<int32, #lvl()>`), and a pick pattern's literals and range bounds. If a
  position cannot hold a substituted expression for a structural reason, say which and why in the report;
  do not leave it MACRO-006.
- **C2 (`mc0046b`; DEF-171).** A body that is nothing but one invocation "is whatever `b` is" (D-125,
  MACRO_REFERENCE §1). In `expand_splice`, before the body-kind check: when the macro's body is the
  expression form and that expression is an invocation (`ExprBuiltinExpr`), the splice emits ONE
  `DeclMacroSplice` holding the cloned invocation (the arguments substituted) at every declaration site —
  module, struct, impl/trait — and the next round expands it as what its target is; a target that does not
  fit the site is refused by the target's own check.
- **C3 (`mc0246`, `mc0251`; DEF-172, DEF-173).** A macro that invokes itself, directly or through others,
  never settles, and that is decided AT THE DECLARATIONS: after `collect_macros`, build the invocation
  graph of each module's macros and refuse every cycle once, `NITPICK-MACRO-004` at the declaration of
  the cycle's first macro in source order, naming the chain: "macro `ping_m` never settles: `ping_m`
  invokes `pong_m`, which invokes `ping_m` (MACRO_REFERENCE §7)". The edges of a macro are the
  `ExprBuiltinExpr` nodes in its body's expression-id range naming a macro of the same module: the parser
  already knows the range (`elo`/`ehi` in the macro-declaration arm — record it on the declaration, e.g.
  two trailing ints of its window with accessors beside `macro_body_at`). A macro body has no conditional,
  so a cycle in this graph is non-termination whenever any member is invoked, and refusing it uninvoked is
  what §7's example says. The driver does not expand after a cycle is reported (the rule it already has
  after a parse error). The ROUND bound stays for a finite chain deeper than 32: it reports at the first
  invocation still standing in program text (not in a macro body) — never at module 0's root, which is
  `prelude.npk:17:1` — naming that macro. A `macro:` declared inside a macro body is refused where it is
  written (`NITPICK-MACRO-005`: macros are collected before expansion, so one emitted by expansion could
  never be invoked) instead of MACRO-006 when the outer one expands. `expansion_notes` is called ONCE (the
  driver calls it twice in a row at one return, so a note is annotated by a second note), and never
  annotates a note.
- **C4 (`mc0085b`).** No compiler change. A program test or accept file pins that a spliced `fixed` field is
  `fixed`, and that an initialiser is still refused (MACRO-005).

**Tests, for every package.** Each refusal has a rejection file in the suite of the stage that refuses
(`tests/types/rejection/`, `tests/analysis/rejection/`, `tests/expansion/rejection/`), ONE `// expect-error:`
+ `// expect-error-at:` pair PER SITE at the top of the file (D-332), measured with the built checker and
READ; controls in the same file. A refusal the LEXER or the PARSER makes (A2's LEX-009) is NEVER a
rejection file — the `grammar` stage parses every file of the tree (D-085) — it is a unit case in
`tests/frontend/` beside the existing lexer cases. Each newly accepted shape has a program in
`tests/backend/programs/` (exit 0 at both legs) or `tests/accept/`. A new code is declared in its
`*_codes.npk` and has its case (`check_codes_tested`). The listener's programs are in
`/home/randy/Workspace/REPOS/nitpick-libs/nitpick-fuzz/m11/programs/<id>.npk` (`.sh` for the two scripts).

### 2.10c Macro substitution is total, and an argument is the caller's (step 3a: DEF-189; part B: DEF-183 on S-120)

Planned by `nitpick-compiler_23` on 2026-10-01 from a whole reading of `expand.npk`'s clone and of the resolver's
hygiene switch, with probes at both legs on `5fbaf4a` and `9efe218`; corrected by `nitpick-compiler_24` at the
hand-off (four questions, below) and by the step's own probes. THE NEXT LANDING after step 3, by the standing
rule: two of its shapes are programs the compiler accepts and answers wrongly.

**What was wrong, measured on `9efe218`.**

| probe | program | answered | right |
|---|---|---|---|
| `s6` (DEF-189) | `fixed int32:N = 2i32; macro:mk = (N) { int32[N]:a = …; … #size_of<int32[N]>() … }; #mk(3i32)` | exit 8, both legs: the parameter in the TYPE never substituted, the module's `N` read | the argument: three elements |
| `s7` | a parameter as a whole value pattern `(K)` beside a module `K` | EMIT-002 | the argument's literal is the pattern |
| `ha4` | `struct:T = {…}; macro:mk = (T) { T:x = T{ v: 7i32 }; … }; #mk(3i32)` | exit 7: the parameter stands where a TYPE'S NAME stands, the argument dropped | refused at the macro's declaration |
| `ha3` | `macro:bump = (N) { int32:N = 1i32; #caller(got) = N + 1i32; }; #bump(40i32)` | exit 41: the body DECLARES `N`, every later `N` is the argument | refused at the macro's declaration |
| `ha1` (DEF-183) | module `shared = 100`, a caller's local `shared = 5`, `#twice(shared)` | exit 200: the ARGUMENT resolves where the macro was written | S-120: 10, or a refusal |
| `ha2` (DEF-183) | the same with a local `n` and no module `n` | RESOLVE-002 | S-120: 10, or the same refusal |

Why: `clone_expr` substituted an identifier that names a parameter, and nothing else did. A type, a pattern, an
attribute, a generic parameter and a variant's value were copied through BY ID — a call's generic window, a cast's
target, a local's type and attributes, a `for` binding, an arm's pattern, a parameter's type, a function's return
type and generics, a field, a variant, a global's type, an item's generics/attributes/extras, an impl's
target/trait/generics, a `Rules` subject — each with a comment saying "copied through … inventing a rule about a
form nobody has written". MACRO_REFERENCE §3 is the rule: an argument replaces EVERY occurrence.

**Part A — the clone is total (no user question). As it landed:**

- **WHAT IS SHARED is settled by one question per node kind** — can two instantiations need it to differ, or does
  a later pass WRITE onto it or key a record by it? (The plan said "a type or a pattern with no expression inside is
  shared"; the hand-off's first question was whether anything writes onto a pattern, and something does: the checker
  records a destructure's binding types on the pattern, `ast_set_pat_payload`, and the resolver keys each binding's
  symbol by it.) So: an expression, a statement, a verification node — cloned, as before; a DECLARATION — cloned,
  every kind (a `for` binding, a generic parameter, a field, a variant and an attribute were one node under every
  instantiation's symbol); a PATTERN — cloned, every kind, the checker's slot empty; a TYPE — shared when it holds
  no expression (nothing writes onto a type node, no table is keyed by one, and the only thing in a type an
  instantiation can change is an expression), rebuilt along the path to an expression otherwise.
- `clone_type` / `clone_type_window` (+ `type_holds_expr`, the suffix run followed in a loop, a window under fuel),
  `clone_pat`, `clone_attr`, `clone_generic`, `clone_param` (a parameter, a `for` binding, a variadic tail and a
  field share one layout), `clone_variant`; a cast's target and a `dyn` cast's window get their own arms ahead of
  the shape dispatch. `clone_flat_decl` is gone.
- **What the clone clones is BLANKED in a detached original** (`blank_type`, `blank_type_window`, `blank_pat`,
  `blank_attr`): step 3 walked a shared node "where it stands" at three places (`blank_tree`, `blank_stmt`,
  `instantiate_expr`'s walk of the invocation's own generic arguments), and all three blank now. The audit's
  `in_body && SEEN` exception and the `#caller`-in-a-shared-position sentence are removed: no instantiation leads
  the walk into a template.
- **What a body cannot do with a parameter's name, refused at the declaration** (`check_macro_params`, from
  `check_macro_decls`): use it as the name of a type, and declare it while also writing it as an expression —
  read off the body's id ranges, which the macro declaration records in its window's tail (expressions since
  step 3; types, patterns, statements and declarations added: `MACRO_TAIL` 2 → 10). One report per parameter per
  macro, at its first such spelling. **The plan refused EVERY declaration of a parameter's name; the sweep
  corrected it.** The library listener's `mc0388` — `macro:m = (N) { func:N = …; }; #m(7i32);`, expected to run —
  appeared in the sweep as newly refused, and it is MACRO_REFERENCE §10's own example of an OPEN question ("may a
  parameter name an emitted declaration? … unimplemented rather than refused … recorded as open rather than
  invented"). Refusing it would have settled a recorded-open language question inside a defect fix. The rule was
  narrowed to the half that answers wrongly — the body declares the name AND writes it as an expression (the
  identifier nodes `clone_expr` replaces; the name inside `#caller(…)` is no use) — and the question is registered
  as **S-123**, the user's. **A NEW CODE,
  `NITPICK-MACRO-011`** — the plan said MACRO-005; that one is about what a body emits and where it lands, this is
  about the body's own names: unrelated mistakes with unrelated fixes, and the code table's own rule gives those
  two codes. A bare name in a type-argument list is a type's name by the parser (D-064 §2), so a compile-time value
  is `simd<int32, (N)>`, and the refusal's sentence says so.
- A range ARGUMENT substituted for a parameter that stands as a whole value pattern is MACRO-005 at the pattern,
  once (the pattern is left without a value so `expand_in_pat`'s own check has nothing to say twice).

**The hand-off's four questions, answered by probes before the clone was written** (`wt/24a/.internal/probes/`):
Q1, one destructuring pattern under two instantiations over `Opt<string>` and `Opt<Two>`, consuming and lending,
either order (`q1a`…`q1d`): right answers, the heap numbers the hand-written twin's — the one reader of the
recorded binding types reads them while its own `pick` is checked. Q2, one `for` binding under two NESTED
instantiations (`q2a`): exit 77, right. Both are cloned anyway, so that this is a fact about the tree. Q3, a type
NAME in a body (`q3a`): CAPTURED by the caller's generic parameter, exit 8 for 4 — **DEF-192**, registered, its own
landing. Q4, blanking: as above.

**Part B — whose scope an ARGUMENT resolves in (S-120, the user's; implement on his word).** D-057's rule 1 speaks
of "an identifier in a macro BODY"; rule 3 says `#caller(NAME)` is "the sole way to reach the call site". An
argument is in neither sentence. `clone_arg`'s own comment says "the argument was written at the invocation site,
so its identifiers are the caller's" — and the resolver then resolves the cloned argument under the expansion's
`HYG_ROOT`, in the module scope.
- **R1 (recommended): an argument is the caller's text and resolves at the invocation site.** `#twice(n)` works;
  `ha1` is 10. `#caller(NAME)` stays the BODY's sole way out; an argument is the caller's way in.
- **R2: everything in an expansion resolves where the macro was written**: an argument identifier whose binding at
  the invocation site differs from its binding in the module scope — or exists only at the site — is REFUSED at
  the argument. No local can ever be passed to a macro.

*The mechanism for R1* (settled here so the executor does not design it): a third mark, `HYG_ARG` (the next free
flag bit after `HYG_SEEN`), set by `clone_arg` on the ROOT of the argument's clone — a mark on the root with a
scope STACK, not `HYG_CALLER` on each identifier, because an argument can declare bindings of its own (a `pick`
expression's arm) that a per-identifier mark would look for at the site. The resolver's one-field `caller_scope`
becomes a stack: `resolve_expr`'s `HYG_ROOT` arm pushes the current scope, resolves the subtree in `module_scope`,
pops; the statement form the same. A `HYG_CALLER` identifier looks in the stack's top. **A `HYG_ARG` subtree is
resolved as if it stood at the invocation site: pop the top `t`, resolve the subtree in scope `t`, push `t` back** —
so inside it the stack is what it was at the site, and an argument that itself carries an outer macro's argument
resolves each part where it was written. With an EMPTY stack (a declaration splice) the subtree resolves in
`module_scope`, not in the emitted declaration's inner scopes, where a parameter of the emitted function could
capture the argument's name. Tests: `ha1` (10), `ha2` (10), a statement macro whose block declares `tmp` invoked
with the caller's `tmp`, an argument holding a `pick` expression whose arm binds a name it then reads, an argument
passed down two macro levels, the declaration-splice capture probe, `#caller` beside each; a rejection case for an
argument naming nothing at the site. *For R2*: one refusal at the argument, the same probes as rejection cases.
An argument landing in a TYPE position is folded by the type resolver's own lookup and not through this stack
(DEF-192's side of it).

**Part B as it landed (2026-10-01, landing 92; the record is at the end of this file).** The mark is COUNTED, not a
bit, because the cases coincide on one node -- four ways the planned bit fails, each found by reading before a line
was written: the clone made fresh nodes with an empty flag word (DEF-221); a body that is exactly an invocation --
an alias, D-125 -- roots two expansions at one node; a body that is exactly a parameter puts a root and a step on
one node in the opposite order to an argument that is itself an invocation; and an argument that is exactly an
enclosing macro's parameter needs two steps on one node. And "an empty stack resolves in the module scope" is not
kept: a declaration macro's argument carries its own mark (`HYG_SITE`), so a step back with no site left is the
compiler's defect and is said.

### 2.10d `comptime` orders strings (step 3b: D-338) — planned 2026-10-01 by `nitpick-compiler_28` on `776383f`

**Measured first** (probes `.internal/probes/q*`, `r*`, `s*`, base = `776383f`, both legs). The listener's `mc0309b` is
`NITPICK-TYPE-004` as registered. Around it: `one() ?! E9` in a `comptime` body is TYPE-004 too (the evaluator folds
through `raw` and through no other unwrap); `comptime(Ordering.Less)` is TYPE-004 and `fixed Ordering:O =
Ordering.Less;` TYPE-035; a `pick` in a `comptime func:` body, either form, is TYPE-004. The run time orders
`"\xC3\xA9"` AFTER `"z"` (unsigned bytes) and reads a `char8` range unsigned. A `pick` tests its arms in source order,
a guard after its pattern; `fall L;` enters arm `L`'s body with no test; an arm after an unguarded `(*)` is PICK-004;
a `pass` inside an expression `pick`'s arm leaves the function; a `string` selector is compared by bytes. THREE
DEFECTS FOUND BY THE PROBES: **DEF-211** — the evaluator's `&&`/`||` fold BOTH operands, so `(d != 0i32) &&
((10i32 / d) > 1i32)` with `d = 0` is TYPE-004 "divides by zero" at compile time where the run time answers `false`
(an over-refusal, never a wrong value: a recursion guarded by `||` meets the depth bound the same way); **DEF-212** —
two value arms with one value (`(1i32) {…}, (1i32) {…}`) are accepted by the checker and the emitted `switch` is
refused by `llc` ("duplicate case value"); **DEF-213** — `fold_const` resets the evaluator's depth, so a `comptime
func:` that recurses through a type's array size (`#size_of<int8[comptime(deep())]>()`) is never bounded and the
compiler stops with exit 3 and no message.

**The value.** `CV_ENUM` (6): `type_id` the enum's type, `num` the variant's POSITION among its declaration's
members — the `position` the one tag rule (`variant_tag_of`) takes. Position is identity: DEF-193 made two variants
of one enum never share a tag, so equal positions and equal tags are one question, and the frontend needs no copy of
the tag rule. A variant that carries a payload, and a variant of a GENERIC enum (its instance is not in the
expression: D-261), are not values here.

**What the evaluator gains, and where each lives (`src/frontend/type_resolve.npk`).**
1. `fold_variant` (the `ExprMemberAccessExpr` arm of `fold_expr`): `Enum.Variant` where the base identifier's
   RECORDED symbol is a non-generic `enum` declaration and the member names a payload-less variant.
2. `fold_binary`: `==`/`!=` on two `CV_ENUM` values of one type whose enum carries no payload anywhere (the
   checker's own condition for a built-in `==`, `equatable`); every other operator on one is not a constant.
3. `fold_string_cmp` (the `ExprMethodCallExpr` arm): a method named `cmp` with ONE argument, the receiver and the
   argument both folding to strings, answers the prelude's `Ordering` — found in the PRELUDE's own scope
   (`SymbolTable.prelude_scope`), never by a lookup from the body's scope — by the order `impl:string:Ord` computes:
   unsigned bytes, then length. Identified by the receiver's VALUE because the evaluator runs on a body the checker
   may not have typed yet; it is sound because `string` has one `cmp` in any program that compiles (a second, from
   the program's own trait, is TYPE-020 at every call, the prelude's own included — measured, `q4`). No other method
   call folds.
4. `?!` and `?|` fold through their operand, as `raw` does: an operand the evaluator folded is a call that
   completed (a body that reaches `fail` does not fold), so the trap `?!` names is never taken, and `?|`'s fallback
   is lazy at run time (`ir_expr.npk`, "the default only evaluates on the error path"). Both spellings (the twin
   rule), because `mc0309b` writes `a.cmp(b) ?! E9`.
5. `fold_pick` — `StmtPickStmt` in `fold_stmt`, `ExprPickExpr` in `fold_expr`, one core: the selector folds to a
   plain integer, a `bool`, a `char`, a `string` or a `CV_ENUM`; the arms are tested IN SOURCE ORDER (the chain's
   order; the switch agrees with it in every program that reaches a binary, PICK-004 and DEF-212's `llc` refusal
   being what stands between); a pattern is a wildcard, a value (folded, and compared only where its kind and type
   agree with the selector's — DEF-178 is open, so a pattern the checker never typed DECLINES instead of
   answering), a range (by the selector's signedness; a `char` and a `uint64` unsigned), or a bind-free variant
   looked up BY NAME in the selector's enum with a two-segment head held to the enum's name (`check_enum_arms`'s
   reading); a `where` guard folds to a `bool`; `give` and `fall` are two new `FoldFlow` states (`gave`,
   `fall_to`) that every block and loop propagates as it propagates a return; `fall L;` runs arm `L`'s body with no
   test, under the pick's own share of the fuel (a `fall` cycle is TYPE-025, never a hang). A binding pattern, a
   struct pattern, `ERR:`, a `tbb`/ternary/fixed-point/float selector, a selector no arm matches: the `pick` is not
   evaluated (the site's own TYPE-004).

**The decisions, each with its reason.**
- **Statements run only inside a `comptime func:` call, so a `pick` EXPRESSION folds only there** (a new counter,
  `TypeResolver.fold_frames`; not `fold_depth`, which a nested `fold_const` resets). An arm is a block of
  statements. Folded where it stands in a run-time body — the emitter asks the folder about every `+ - *` node
  (`emit_const_fold`) — an arm's assignment to a local of that body would be "evaluated" into the resolver's top
  environment and never run: `1i32 + pick (2i32) { (2i32) { acc = 7i32; give 20i32; }, … }` would become the
  constant 21 and `acc` stay 0. So outside a frame a `pick` is not a constant, no existing program's emission can
  move for it, and a constant site that wants one calls a `comptime func:` — the marker that says the compiler may
  run code (D-130: "whether the compiler runs your code is not a thing to discover by accident").
- **An arm of the expression form ends in `give`; a `pass` out of one is not evaluated.** It leaves the FUNCTION
  through an expression, and `fold_expr` answers a value, not a flow: carrying a pending return across every
  expression site is a second control channel whose one missed check hands a caller its callee's value. Declined
  (TYPE-004 at the forcing site), stated in MACRO_REFERENCE §8, as `break`/`continue`/`for`/`when` are.
- **An enum value does not leave the evaluator in this landing**: `comptime(expr)` whose value is one is TYPE-004
  with its own sentence (`type_comptime`), and a module binding initialised with one stays TYPE-035
  (`const_init_verdict`, verdict 1) — exactly what both are today. D-338 adds the value to the EVALUATOR and
  "nothing else"; a module-level enum constant is a language addition (D-165's list has none), put to the user as
  **S-127** with the recommendation to admit it, in both positions, as its own landing.
- **DEF-211 is fixed here**: `&&`/`||` decide on the left operand where it decides, as the run time's branch does.
  A guard is where it bites (`where ((n != 0i32) && ((10i32 / n) > 1i32))`), and the fix is three lines in the one
  function the guard goes through.
- **DEF-213 is fixed here**: `fold_const` keeps the evaluation's depth and its said-flag when it is entered from
  inside a frame (`fold_frames > 0`), so the recursion through a type meets the depth bound (TYPE-025) like any
  other. The counter that carries the first decision is what makes it one test.
- **DEF-212 is registered, not fixed**: it is the exhaustiveness analysis's (PICK-004 for a value an earlier
  unguarded arm already took), step 3d's.

**Tests.** Two programs, every case asked at COMPILE time (`comptime(c_f(…))`), at RUN time (`r_f` over arguments
from a function the compiler cannot fold) and against the literal answer — every function written TWICE with one
body, `comptime func:c_f` and `func:r_f`, because a `comptime func:` is emitted nowhere and cannot be called at run
time (DEF-214, found writing them; S-128): `comptime_string_order.npk` (twenty pairs: the empty string, a prefix, equal
strings, bytes on both sides of 0x80, a multi-byte character, `mc0309b`'s pair and its own spelling; enum values held,
returned and compared; `?!` and `?|`; two module constants computed from the order) and `comptime_pick.npk` (seventy
cases: every selector kind; value, negated, range inclusive and exclusive, `uint64` past 2^63, `char8` past 0x7F,
string, variant and wildcard arms; a binding arm passed over; guards with the short-circuit; `fall`; nested picks; the
expression form in arithmetic; locals in arms; a pick in a loop; and `effect`, the run-time `pick` with an assignment
in its arm inside a `+` node — the first decision's control). Rejection: `tests/types/rejection/comptime_enum_value.npk`
(TYPE-004 at an enum value forced out, twice, and at each declined shape — a `pick` under `comptime(…)` outside a
frame, a `pass` out of an expression arm, a `tbb` selector, `cmp` on integers, a generic enum's variant; TYPE-035 at a
module binding and ONE TYPE-004 at a module binding forced through `comptime(…)`; TYPE-025 for two arms that fall to
each other; four controls that fold) and `comptime_depth_through_type.npk` (DEF-213: TYPE-025 once, with a control).
Each new test is run under the PARENT compiler too, which must fail it.

**Measurements.** The standing chain on `776383f`'s binaries: both sweeps (tree + libraries + applications), the
emission comparison (no existing program's text is meant to move: every differing pair is READ — the one road is
`(comptime_call ?! E) + k`, whose `+` node folds now), the libraries' emission, D-332's count, the verify-row
hashes, the program suite at both legs, the unit suites, `vprog`, then `record_chain` (the two zeros).

**Not in this landing.** An enum value leaving the evaluator (S-127). A `pass` out of an expression arm. DEF-212.
Any method call but `cmp` on two strings. Advance notice F32 names what moves: `mc0309b` to `run:0`, TYPE-004
removed at the forms above, and whatever the sweep finds.

### 2.11 DEF-154 — the ninety-four documentation rows (step 4)

## 3. Questions for the user (each with a recommendation; the recap of what they rest on)

- **S-115 — which child's error a join relays.** D-163's exception (settled with the user
  at 1.1's planning) and CONCURRENCY_REFERENCE §2.2 say the enclosing scope's join relays
  "the first child error, verbatim (D-080), after every child has finished"; D-207 (1.4.4)
  moved the join into the scope's unwind and kept the arbitration reading one slot. The
  emitter relays the LAST-spawned child's error (the join walks the LIFO chain and keeps
  the first error it meets). **Recommended: spawn order** — the earliest-spawned child that
  failed — because it is a function of the program's text and holds under every schedule
  the explorer runs; "first in time" needs a stamp the floor does not keep and would let one
  program relay two errors on two runs. Lands at step 1 under the recommendation unless the
  user says otherwise.

## 5. Decisions taken at planning (each with its reason)

- **Step 1 before 1.6.1d's 3b and 4** (the user, 2026-09-26): four silent wrong answers and
  a use-after-free in safe code outrank a leak and the over-restrictions.
- **The emitter resolves an identifier by the resolver's symbol, not by its name.** Two
  resolvers of one name disagree exactly where hygiene matters; one authority (the resolver's
  record on the node) is the blueprint rule, and the by-declaration slot lookup already
  exists for D-286's guard sites.
- **A `\u{…}` escape is `char32` whatever its value; a source character above 0xFF too.** The
  reference says the first; the second is the same defect (a code point truncated in silence)
  decided the safe way, and stated in the reference.
- **Spawn order for the join (recommended, S-115).** Deterministic under every schedule.
- **The expired wait fails without re-acquiring.** D-056's amendment: holding a lock past an
  expired deadline is the unbounded acquire the decision forbids; the guard is spent.
- **A spawn's crossing is a lent party, not a claim.** A `$$i` claim refuses every
  write-capable access, and the arena's `alloc` is one the contract permits; a party kind
  with the type's concurrent set exempted says exactly what D-154 and D-180 say.
- **The depth bound is the parser's, at 256.** One rule at the door where the analyses each
  keep their own; 256 is their figure, and the folder's stack lasts to 450.
- **The build mode lives in the checker.** A mode only the driver knows has no suite; the
  rejection suites drive `tools/check.npk`, so the flag reaches it and a header marker names
  it.
- **The `cstring` literal joins 3b.** Its type's shape changes there; deciding it twice
  would decide it wrong once.

## 6. What the executor should know before starting

- **Reproduce first, at both legs**, with the listener's programs (`.internal/repro.sh -s`
  over `findings/F-0NN*/*.npk`); every fix's program measured before and after.
- **Both checkers for every refusal that moves**: the base is step 3's (`.internal/base/`
  in the worktree, built from `3235308` before any edit); `sweep.sh` + `sweep_diff.py`
  over the tree and the listener's repositories; the listener's tree moves between sweeps.
- **`ANY src/ change moves nitpick.obligations`**: `npkg verify --record` in every commit,
  the gate's two zeros, re-keyed rows classified as moves.
- **A change to emitted text breaks the emitter's unit tests**: `harness.py --only
  tests/backend/` before every commit of step 1 (the identifier lookup, the join, the wait).
- **Explored programs' kept seeds go stale with an IR change on their steps** (DEF-67):
  after DEF-146 and DEF-147, run the `explore` stage's units by hand (`xprog.py`) for
  `sync_prims`, `trap_one_failsafe`, the join tests, and keep or re-seed.
- **The harness takes about three hours and prints nothing for most of it**; check the
  process, not the log. Each step in its own worktree stacked on the previous commit.
- **The M11 programs' failsafes name every arm**; a probe of one's own needs the same list
  (REACH-002 otherwise: the depth probes of this planning failed on that and not on depth).

## 7. Acceptance for the subcycle

Every one of the listener's 26 programs answers the reference's exit at both legs (or is
refused with the code the finding names); every new rejection file its exact set; the
compiler's own build under every new rule; the sweep's every moved site recorded by shape;
the manifest gate's two zeros at every commit; the harness green with `parity` agreeing at
every landing; the four notices sent, each with its ladder rows; the sixteen rows decided
and the ninety-four sentences corrected; OPEN_DECISIONS §4's eleven entries FIXED (DEF-155
and DEF-156 registered); the listener told the F → DEF mapping (done 2026-09-26) and each
step's exposure before it lands.

## Execution record (appended as the steps land)

### Step 1 — LANDED 2026-09-26 (`nitpick-compiler_20`; DEF-144…DEF-148 fixed; DEF-157 and DEF-158 found by the step's probes and fixed; DEF-159 registered)

**Reproduced first** on step 3's compiler (`3235308`, `.internal/repro.sh` at both legs): every one of the
sixteen programs of F-018…F-022 answered as the listener's VERDICTS say (h1/h2 10, u1 10, u2 TYPE-007, j1 10,
ctl_j3 0, t1 10, a1 96; every control 0), and their M11 claim programs the same way (`cc0080`, `cc0321`,
`cc0568b`, `mc0188`, `mc0202`, `ty0272`, `ty0280`).

**DEF-144 — the emitter reads the resolver's symbol.** `ident_slot` (ir_expr.npk): a `SYM_STMT` symbol by its
declaring statement (`fnem_lookup_decl`, the by-declaration lookup D-286 added; by name only where no slot is
recorded under the statement — a `for` binding), a parameter by the prologue's binding (`fnem_lookup_param`, a
forward search over the table's parameter prefix, so an expansion's own local never shadows `#caller(p)`), a
function, an error constant or a global to `emit_decl_value` before any local is consulted, every other
declaration kind (a variadic collector's spec) and a `SYM_PAT` symbol by name. The six sites that asked the name
ask it: `emit_ident`, `addr_of_place`, `clear_root_flag`, `emit_assign`'s target flag, `place_root_local`; the
guard site's holder lookup was already by declaration. **Found on the way:** the first form returned "no slot" for
every non-parameter `SYM_DECL`, and a variadic collector (`..*int64[]:rest`, a `DeclVariadicSpec` symbol bound by
the prologue under its name) reached `llc` as EMIT-002 — `variadic.npk`, caught by the emission comparison's
`new-refused` column; the default is the by-name reading for every declaration kind but the three that are
values (DEF-54's lesson). `emit_call` needed nothing: it has resolved a callee by the node's symbol since DEF-54.

**DEF-145 — a character literal has a width**, carried as the numeric literals carry theirs: the lexer writes
`WChar32` into the token's `width` for the `\u{…}` escape (the byte after the backslash is `u`) and for a code
point above 0xFF, `WChar8` otherwise; the parser hands it to the node (`ast_add_expr_w`); the typer, the folder
and the emitter read `char_lit_bits` — ONE token kind and one node kind, where the plan wrote two token kinds
(the width field already existed for the numeric literals, and a second kind for one literal form is two
spellings of one fact). `char_lit_bits` lives in parse_expr.npk beside `expr_num_width`: its first home,
num_width.npk, is written whole by `gen_tables.py`, and the in-process checks refused the tree
(`check_generated_current`) before anything else ran. No verify test, no `src/`, `lib/` or `npkg/` file and no
file of the listener's holds a `\u{…}` character literal (the census: zero).

**DEF-146 — the join relays the earliest-spawned child's error (S-115's recommendation, ratified as D-334 the
same day — the user: "I am fine with the s-115 thing. go with what we have unless there is a compelling case
not to do so").** `emit_scope_join`
keeps two words per join, `jtook` (`i8`) and `jbare` (`i32`), hoisted allocas (the walk pumps the executor
synchronously and never suspends the frame): a child's error overwrites one a previous child of the walk stored
and never the function's own (the slot's error is the function's own exactly when it is non-zero and `jtook` is
0); the origin chain records the function once per walk, at the first child error taken. **A departure from the
plan's letter, for the rule's uniformity:** the plan kept `main`'s (a bare function's) trap path as it was — the
first error the LIFO walk met, straight into `failsafe` — which would have relayed the LAST-spawned child's
error from `main` and the earliest-spawned from every other function, a meaning that changes with context;
`main`'s join stores each child's error into `jbare` and, after `jn.end`, enters `failsafe` with the earliest-
spawned child's, every child joined (`join_main_first_spawned.npk`: 82).

**DEF-147 — an expired wait fails without re-acquiring.** At `emit_condvar_wait`'s `reacq`: `npk_mono_now()`
against the absolute deadline; at or past it, `-4107` into the error slot and a branch to `fin` (the frame off the
condvar's list, the lent guard nulled, the mutex untouched); the re-acquire's own `-4107` and its park-and-retry
unchanged. No floor byte. `sync_prims.npk`'s waiter loop swallows the wait's error with `?! E9` and still
answers 42 (its signaller flips the predicate within the wait's second).

**DEF-148 — a spawn lends its crossing until the block's join.** alias.npk: `PARTY_LENT`, pushed by
`lend_spawn_args` at the end of `acc_call` for a call that is the operand of a `drop` whose callee is `async`
(`callee_is_async`, `pub` in escape.npk, remembered on the walk as `spawn_call`), one party per argument that is
a pointer to one of D-180's five kinds (`@place`, or a pointer binding handed on — `root_ptr`, so the binding's
own reassignment is no conflict), sited at the call, live until the enclosing block's `frame_pop`;
`conflicts(LENT, WRITE|EXCLUSIVE)`; the receiver of a builtin method in the kind's concurrent set is checked
with the lent parties skipped (`skip_lent`, for that one access); `@s` to a known callee is judged by the
callee's mutation summary as a view is (`check_written_mode`; `CHECK_VIEWS` covers the summary-judged kinds,
`CHECK_CLAIMS` skips them, `CHECK_LENT` is the spawn's own arguments read against their own lent party — a task
that stores over what it was lent is refused at the spawn); the report is `report_lent_conflict`, BORROW-016.
The two tables — `spawn_crossing_kind` (D-180's five, which `escape_spawn_args` reads too now) and
`crossing_concurrent_method` — are escape.npk's, one authority. **Found on the way:** the "a party never
conflicts with the access that made it" skip (a range view's own creation) is a VIEW party's alone now — a lent
party's place IS the spawn's `@s` operand, and the skip hid the spawn's own callee from its own party (the
seventh site of `spawn_lent.npk`). The escape analysis's summaries needed no change: a builtin method records no
write (the plan's "stop recording `alloc`/`get`" was already so), and `destroy` through a pointer is refused
outright (DEF-157), so no callee can end a lent value's life except by storing over the pointee — which the
summary records.

**DEF-157 — `destroy` through a pointer double-frees (found by DEF-148's probes).** `kill(shared_arena<int64>->:s)
{ s.destroy(); }` called with `@s` from a returning function: `MachineFault` (107) at both legs on `3235308`; the
consuming half of `destroy` (D-152) clears the receiver BINDING's flag, and through a pointer there is none.
`destroy_receiver_owned` (type_members.npk) walks the receiver to its root through member and index bases that
are not pointers, slices or handles and accepts a local or a parameter of this function, else
`NITPICK-TYPE-091` — a pointer parameter's pointee, a dereference, a pointee's field, a temporary — for both
arena kinds. The census: every `.destroy()` in the tree and in the listener's repositories takes a binding or a
local struct's field.

**DEF-158 — `destroy` on a field cleared the aggregate's flag (the same probes).** `app.store.destroy()` on
`App{ string:name; shared_arena<int64>:store; }` leaked `app.name` at every scope exit — peak 72,592 bytes over

### DEF-193 — LANDED 2026-10-01 (`nitpick-compiler_24`; worktree `wt/24b`, branch `24-161-1e-193` on `c6d671d`; landing 85; S-122 the user's)

**The defect** (registered at step 3a, found writing DEF-189's variant-value case): `variant_tag_of` has the whole
rule — "the declared integer LITERAL where one is given, the POSITION otherwise" — and `check_decl` had no arm for
an enum, so nothing held a declaration to it. A value written as an expression was ignored in silence
(`enum:B = { X = 7i32 + 1i32; Y = 2i32; };` — `B.X =>! int32` is 0; probe `ev3`), two variants could carry one tag
(`ev5`: `F.A == F.B` true; `ev4`: `llc`, "duplicate case value in switch"), a literal past `int32` was truncated.

**The fix.** `check_enum_values` (type_stmt.npk) behind a `DeclEnumDecl` arm of `check_decl`: `NITPICK-TYPE-093`
(`TYPE_ENUM_VALUE`, new) at the value for (1) a value that is not a bare `ExprIntLiteral`, (2) a value outside
`[0, 2^31 − 1]`, (3) a tag an earlier variant already has — the message naming the earlier variant and, where one
of the two has no explicit value, saying that its tag is its position. A refused value takes no part in the
duplicate check (D-240). The position counts every member, as the emitter's does.

**Probed before it was measured** (`wt/24b/.internal/probes`): a literal past the 64-bit envelope is the lexer's
(`NITPICK-LEX-004`), so the node's payload is always the value; `5`, `6u8`, `0FFhex` are literals (the suffix is
not read); `'a'` and `true` are refused as non-integer literals; a payload variant takes no `= value` (PARSE-001);
an enum inside an inline module, a generic enum and a macro-emitted enum are each checked, the last after
expansion (a value that is a macro argument is judged as what it became, with the MACRO-009 note).

**The test.** `tests/types/rejection/enum_values.npk` — TYPE-093 ×12 (four non-literal values, two out of range of
which one would have collided once truncated and is reported ONCE, five shared tags, one inside an inline module)
and seven controls (unvalued, valued, sparse, mixed without a collision, a value that is a macro invocation, a
parenthesised literal, a generic enum with payloads); the header measured to a fixed point and read against its
lines. The positive controls already in the tree: `enums_pick.npk` (`Late = 9i32` among unvalued variants, read
back), `macro_param_positions.npk` (a value from a macro argument).

### DEF-192 and DEF-196 — LANDED 2026-10-01 (`nitpick-compiler_24`; worktree `wt/24c`, branch `24-161-1e-192` on `2e41059`; landing 86; S-124 the user's)

**DEF-192, read for its fix.** The type checker resolves a type's name itself (`resolve_user_named`): the generic
parameters of the declaration it stands in (`r.generics`, then `r.enclosing`), then the enclosing trait's associated
types, then the scope — and a macro body lands inside the declaration that invoked it. Probed on `c6d671d`: the
invoking function's parameter captures (`q3a`: 8 for 4), the invoking `impl`'s too (`q3d`: 8 for 4), an associated
type does not (`q3e`), and a file-level macro cannot be invoked inside an inline module (`q4a`: MACRO-007), so no
scope captures. Where the module holds no type of the spelling the capture is what made the body compile.

**The fix.** A node is asked which macro body it was WRITTEN in by its span: the parser records each macro
declaration's extent (`ast_note_macro` → `Ast.macro_spans`: file, first token's offset, the offset past its `;`),
and `ast_macro_at(ast, span)` answers 0 or the declaration's ordinal — a clone keeps its template's span, so the
body's own type nodes (shared by every instantiation where they hold no expression) and an instantiation's copies
answer alike. `macro_may_bind(written, declared)` is the rule, asked at `generic_bind_in`'s name match and before
`assoc_of_trait`: a name written in macro `m` binds what is declared in `m` and nothing else that is not a scope's;
a name written outside every macro binds as it did (an argument's type names are the invocation's; what an
argument's IDENTIFIERS see is S-120). An id range could not carry it (it knows the template's nodes and not their
copies) and a flag would have had to be threaded through every constructor of every node kind.

**DEF-196, found reading `fold_ident` for the above.** `FoldEnv` was one flat (name, value) list per `comptime func:`
call; its comment said "there is no nested scope in a body this evaluator accepts". Probed on `c6d671d`: `q5f` (a
nested block's local overwrites the outer one: 12 for 11), `c1` (the same over a parameter: 50 for 3), `q5a` (a
macro body's free name reads a `comptime` local: 6 for 105), `q5c`/`q5d` (a name inside a type reads a local where
the checker reads the module's `fixed` binding: 8 for 12). **The fix:** `FoldEnv` is `{ kinds, origins, vals }`,
keyed by the symbol's origin; `fold_ident` asks only where the identifier has a symbol; the assignment arm writes
its target's symbol; nothing is asked by name. The first form kept a by-name fallback for a name inside a type —
withdrawn the same hour, when the control (`q5d`, no macro) showed the checker reading the MODULE's binding there
(D-222): the fallback was the wrong answer itself.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/macro_type_hygiene.npk` (eight cases: the two captures, and what the rule
must keep — an emitted generic function's, struct's and `impl`'s own parameters, an argument naming the caller's
parameter, an argument written inside another body, a body invoked inside another body's generic function);
`tests/types/rejection/macro_type_hygiene.npk` (TYPE-001 ×3 with their MACRO-009 notes: `#size_of<U>()` under
`func:measure<U>`, a field spliced into `struct:Box<V>`, `#size_of<W>()` in a method of `impl:<W>:Holder<W>`; two
controls); `tests/backend/programs/comptime_scopes.npk` (twelve cases, the run-time twins beside the compile-time
ones). Each is wrong or accepted on `2e41059`: the programs exit 1, the rejection file is silent but for its
missing `failsafe`.

**The measurements** (base = `2e41059`'s binaries, side by side). The sweep over 3,601 files:
1 site vanished and 6 appeared, every one in the new rejection test (its REACH-003 under the old checker; its three TYPE-001 with their three notes) — no file of nitpick-libs or nitpick-apps moves, and none of the tree's own: nothing anywhere compiled by the capture; per code REACH-003 4 → 3, TYPE-001 46 → 49, the note MACRO-009 26 → 29 (2,393 → 2,398 sites); no TRAP or TIMEOUT. The
emission comparison: 538 programs of the tree, 536 byte-identical (the 2 that differ are the two new programs, each answering wrongly on the parent); and over
the libraries and applications, every `.npk` holding a `main` (2,592): 1,562 compile under both compilers
