### DEF-226 — LANDED 2026-10-09 (`nitpick-compiler_35`; worktree `wt/35c`, branch `35-def226` on landing 109's commit; landing 110; found by landing 93's probes)

**Where it started.** Landing 93 (DEF-225: a bare `#unreachable();` leaves) probed the early exits a program writes before
reading a `Result`'s value and found the `pick` form refused where the `if` form compiles: `pick (r.is_error) { (true) {
exit 10i32; }, (false) { } }` followed by `r.value` is `NITPICK-TAINT-001`, with every leaver in the error arm -- `exit`,
`!!! Unreachable;`, the bare `#unreachable();` DEF-225 made a leaver -- on the compiler before landing 93 and after it alike
(`p06b`, `p06c`). Registered 2026-10-01 as DEF-226, a refusal of a correct program, with the reading that settles it: `checked`
is a PERMISSION and intersects, so the step is `must`'s twin under the same exhaustiveness condition, and a wrong answer
towards `checked` admits a tainted read (D-007). MEASURED FIRST on landing 108's compiler: the three leavers TAINT-001 after
the pick, and a pick whose error arm falls through TAINT-001 (`scratchpad/def226/p_*.npk`).

**The defect.** `assign_pick` (bindings.npk) runs every arm from the pristine pre-pick state, intersects the states of the
arms that fall through into an accumulator (`state_intersect_must`: `must` and `checked` both by AND, the may-facts by OR)
and, for a TOTAL pick, takes the accumulator's facts out of the pick -- and `state_take_must` took `must` alone. D-121
promised the `pick` form "the same refinement an `if` gets"; the refinement was marked on the safe arm
(`state_mark_checked` where the arm's value is the one that means no error) and held inside it, and the merge dropped it
while keeping definite assignment through the same construct (`unreachable_leaves.npk`'s `assigned_pick`).

**The fix.** `state_take_definite`, one function where `state_take_must` was: `must` and `checked` both leave a total
pick, each the intersection over the arms that fall through, for one reason -- every path out of a total pick ran some
arm that falls through, so a fact true at the end of every such arm is true after the pick. The error arm that LEAVES
contributes nothing to the intersection (it is not a path out), so the safe arm's check survives; the error arm that
falls through unchecked keeps the read tainted, which is the control.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/pick_checked_leaver.npk`: the `exit` leaver in `main`, `!!! Unreachable;` and
`#unreachable();` in two functions, the value read after each pick -- exit 0 at both legs, three TAINT-001 on landing
108's compiler; `tests/accept/taint.npk` gains `pick_leaver_trap` and `pick_leaver_unreachable` beside its `pick_form`
(two TAINT-001 before); `tests/analysis/rejection/taint.npk` gains `pick_error_arm_falls_through` with its own
expectation line (seven sites, seven lines). DECISIONS carries a dated note on D-121; OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-226 FIXED;
the 1.6 README's Remaining list drops it. The same commit carries, in OPEN_DECISIONS' S-134 row, the library seat's
correction of landing 109's advance notice (a macro is invocable only in its declaring module, D-124, so nitpick-posix's
PX-010 had given way to PX-100's generated `failsafe.npk` per utility on 2026-09-03, and D-352 removes one of PX-100's two
reasons), its three ideas for the question, and the author's instruction that the planning weigh every idea, theirs and
ours, for the best outcome for everybody -- each verbatim.

**The measurements** (base = landing 109's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 109's checker and this one over 5,118
files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 5 sites vanished
(the three TAINT-001 of `pick_checked_leaver.npk` and the two of `tests/accept/taint.npk`'s new cases), 0 appeared -- 4,199 → 4,194; per code NITPICK-TAINT-001 19 -> 14. THE EMISSION
COMPARISON: 569 programs under both compilers, 568 byte-identical, 0 different, 0
newly refused, 0 refused by both, and one refused by the base alone -- the landing's own
`pick_checked_leaver.npk`. The libraries and applications: 4,003 programs, 1,844 byte-identical, 2,159
refused by both, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by the base
alone. D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The verify
suite's problem text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133 files
PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 386 programs at both legs, 377 at their expected exit, the
nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests:
the frontend's 35 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree — 7,070 obligations (3,427 discharged, 2,728 open, 0 budget, 910 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the
verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations` 6,774 rows
before and 6,774 after, 6,769 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, 2 (symbol,
kind) discharged counts fell -- a RENAME: `state_take_must`'s `overflow` 2 -> 0 and `terminate` 2 -> 0 are `state_take_definite`'s 2 and 2; the 5 rows not shared are the RENAMED function's: `state_take_must`'s 2 `overflow` discharged, 1 `overflow` open and 2 `terminate` discharged are `state_take_definite`'s, verdict for verdict (the copy loop's `i + 1` and its measure; the added `checked` line carries no row), and no type-id move: every other row is shared, the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests differing by exactly the rename (5 added, 5 removed); the floor's 388 unmoved. The in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A REFUSAL REMOVED: a `Result` checked by a total `pick (r.is_error)` whose error arm leaves
is checked after the pick, where its read was `NITPICK-TAINT-001`; no library or application program moves. No emission moves for a program
that compiled before (the analysis feeds refusals alone); no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced
in advance (NOTICES F52).

### DEF-233 and DEF-238 — LANDED 2026-10-09 (built by `nitpick-compiler_35` and `nitpick-compiler_36`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_37` on 2026-10-10 (this said "landed by `nitpick-compiler_36`" until landing 112: the texts were written at the commit, 2026-10-09 23:48, before the hand-off; `nitpick-compiler_36` added the two faces and committed, `nitpick-compiler_37` landed it on 2026-10-10 02:19 after the harness came back green); worktree `wt/35d`, branch `35-def233` on landing 110's commit; landing 111; the library listener's F-038 and F-046 in O-N36)

**Where it started.** The listener's M11 session, reported 2026-10-07 (O-N36): a `give` or a `fall` outside a `pick` arm
made `npkc` exit 3 with no message (F-038, DEF-233), and a frac's `.num` assignment passed the checker and was EMIT-002
(F-046 (a), DEF-238 (a)); F-046 (b), a `fixed` parameter reassigned inside its callee, was DEF-248's shape and landed at
102. Both are the one class a safety compiler must not have: a stop or a refusal by the wrong tool where the checker owes a
sentence. MEASURED FIRST on landing 108's compiler (`scratchpad/def2xx/`): `give x;` and `fall two;` in a plain body exit 3
with 0 bytes of stderr -- gdb puts the trap at `ir_func.fnem_pick_slot` under `ir_stmt.emit_give`, the emitter's lookup for
the enclosing pick's slot; `f.num = 1i32;` on a `frac32` is EMIT-002 at the assignment.

**The defects.** The checker's statement walk (`check_stmt`, type_stmt.npk) let a `fall` stand anywhere by a deliberate
"nothing to type" (it carries a label and no expression) and a `give` by the generic operand walk, so each reached the
emitter, where only an enclosing pick gives it meaning. And the place question (`require_place`, `require_place_operand`)
walked a member access to its ROOT: a `frac`'s `.whole`/`.num`/`.denom` are typed as VALUES by the member typer (D-198:
read-only views of one value, so the invariants cannot be broken through them), but `f.num` on a local walks to `f` and
passed, and the emitter had nothing to store into.

**The fixes.** `NITPICK-TYPE-094`: the expression typer counts the pick arms that enclose the statement being checked
(`arm_depth`, raised around the arm body in the statement pick's `StmtPickArm` case and in the expression pick's arm loop)
and the pick EXPRESSION arms among them (`givex_depth`); `check_stmt` refuses a `fall` at depth 0 and a `give` outside a pick
expression's arm (a statement pick's arm gives nothing, WHATEVER ENCLOSES IT: the arm zeroes `givex_depth` for its body, so
the innermost pick governs for `give` as it does for `fall`); and inside a `defer` body either one is `NITPICK-TYPE-040`, the
cleanup rule's (D-163 rule 7, beside `fail` and `relay`), asked first. TWO MORE FACES OF THE SAME DEFECT were found by the
landing seat's probes of the first form before the record, on landing 110's compiler and the first form's alike: a `give` in
a statement pick's arm NESTED in a pick expression's arm passed the first form (the outer depth carried into the nested
arm) and was EMIT-002 (the emitter met the statement pick's empty slot), and a `give` or a `fall` in a `defer` body written
in an arm still exited 3 in silence (the emitter writes the body at the scope's exits, when the pick is no longer open, and
`fnem_pick_slot` indexed an empty stack). Measured with them, at both legs: a `give` from inside an `if` or a loop in an arm
reaches its pick, a `fall` from inside a loop reaches its arm, a pick expression nested inside a statement pick's arm inside
a pick expression's arm gives to its own pick, and an owning local the `give` leaves behind is dropped (the arm's frames
unwind through `run_defers_down_to`; `peak_live` 6 at two churn sizes). `NITPICK-TYPE-024` by name for a `frac` member standing where a
place is needed -- "a frac is written whole, by its arithmetic or a cast, never through a member" -- one question
(`frac_member_target`, type_expr.npk: the base's recorded type is a frac), asked by the assignment's place check (the target
is typed first) and, because the `@`, `$$i`/`$$m` and `move` typers ask the place question BEFORE typing their operand, asked
again by each of them once the operand is typed (`refuse_frac_member`; one report: the second asker finds the first's 0).
The first form missed `@g.denom` for exactly that order, and the test found it before the chain ran.

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/give_fall_outside.npk`: seven sites -- a `give` in a plain body, a `fall` in a plain
body, a `give` inside a STATEMENT pick's arm, a `give` in each arm of a statement pick nested in a pick expression's arm
(TYPE-094), a `give` and a `fall` inside a `defer` body written in an arm (TYPE-040) -- beside the controls (a `give` in an
expression pick's arm, a `fall` in each spelling's arm, a `give` through an `if` and a loop, a pick expression nested inside a
statement pick's arm inside a pick expression's arm); `tests/types/rejection/frac_member_write.npk`: five sites -- an assignment, a compound assignment, `@`,
`$$m`, `$$i` -- beside the reads, which compile; both headers regenerated to a fixed point from the checker's report
(`header_from_check.py`). CONTROL_REFERENCE 1.2.2 gains the sub-bullet and §4.5 the `defer` sentence, DECISIONS' D-163 rule 7 a
dated note, TYPE_REFERENCE's frac members bullet the sentence; OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-233 FIXED and DEF-238 FIXED with (b)
recorded as DEF-248's; the 1.6 README's Remaining list drops both.

**The measurements** (base = landing 110's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 110's checker and this one over 5,120 files
(the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 17 sites appeared (the five TYPE-094 and two TYPE-040 of `give_fall_outside.npk` and the five TYPE-024 of `frac_member_write.npk`; and the listener's own probes of the two findings, refused by name where the compiler stopped or the emitter refused: F-038's `c1_give_outside_pick.npk` (a `give` under an `if` in `main`) and `c2_fall_outside_pick.npk` (a bare `fall`), the fuzzer's `as0202.npk` and `as0203.npk` (AST_REFERENCE §2.2's claims, "expect: refuse") -- TYPE-094 each -- and `ty1649.npk` (`a.num = 2i32;` on a frac, TYPE_REFERENCE's read-only members, "expect: refuse"), TYPE-024),
2 vanished (the base checker's REACH-001 at the two new rejection files' bare `failsafe`s -- the base accepts their bodies and reaches the reach analysis, which this compiler's refusals now precede) -- 4,196 → 4,211; per code NITPICK-REACH-001 7 -> 5; NITPICK-TYPE-024 6 -> 12; NITPICK-TYPE-040 2 -> 4; NITPICK-TYPE-094 0 -> 9. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 569
programs under both compilers, 569 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0
refused by both. The libraries and applications: 4,003 programs, 1,844 byte-identical, 2,159 refused by
both, 0 different, 0 newly refused. D-332's count over the five rejection suites:
0 mismatched files. The verify suite's problem text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both
compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 386 programs at both legs,
377 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The
frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's 35 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at
this tree — 7,076 obligations (3,427 discharged, 2,734 open, 0 budget, 910 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker;
`nitpick.obligations` 6,774 rows before and 6,780 after, 6,662 shared, ZERO verdicts moved
among shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell; the 6 rows added are the two depth counters' arithmetic -- `type_pick_expr`'s 4 `overflow` open (`arm_depth` and `givex_depth` raised and lowered around an expression pick's arm) and `check_stmt`'s 2 (`arm_depth` around a statement pick's arm) -- and the 112 rows not shared are RE-KEYED, a type-id move in 27 functions of `alias`, `ir_expr`, `ir_types`, `numeric`, `pipeline`, `smt_encode` and `smt_writer` (the same rows per function, verdict for verdict) beside the two functions that gained rows, the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests differing by exactly the 6 added; the floor's 388 unmoved.
The in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** TWO REFUSALS ADDED, each a stop or a wrong tool's refusal moved to the checker: `NITPICK-TYPE-094`
at a `fall` outside any `pick` arm or a `give` outside a `pick` expression's arm, a statement pick's arm nested in one included
(the compiler exited 3 in silence, or the emitter refused); `NITPICK-TYPE-024` at a `frac` member standing where a place is needed
(EMIT-002); and `NITPICK-TYPE-040` extends to a `give` or a `fall` inside a `defer` body (exit 3 in silence); no library or application program is refused anew. No emission moves for a program that
compiles on both; no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in advance (NOTICES F53).

**The landing's own path, for the record.** Built, tested and documented by `nitpick-compiler_35` in `wt/35d` on landing 109's

### DEF-232 — LANDED 2026-10-15 (built by `nitpick-compiler_35`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_38` (this said "landed by `nitpick-compiler_37`" until landing 113: the texts were written at the commit, 2026-10-15 08:29, before the 09:00 hand-off; `nitpick-compiler_37` rebased, chained and recorded it, `nitpick-compiler_38` landed it on 2026-10-15 10:48 after the harness came back green); worktree `wt/35e`, branch `35-def232` on landing 111's commit; landing 112; the library listener's F-048 in O-N36)

**Where it started.** The listener's M11 session (O-N36, 2026-10-07): a struct with a `NIL` field is accepted by the checker and
emitted as `type { i32, void }`, which `llc` and `opt` refuse -- "void type only allowed for function results" -- while the control
without the field runs. Loud, but the wrong tool refusing a program the language admits: TYPE_REFERENCE says "a `NIL` struct field
takes no space" and D-084 that `NIL` is zero-sized, so the field is legal and the emitter owed it a lowering. MEASURED FIRST on
landing 111's compiler (`scratchpad/def232/`): the literal, a module constant and a generic instance at `NIL` (`Box<NIL>`) each
reach `llc` and are refused for the `void`; an enum whose payload is `NIL` (`enum:E = { A(NIL); B; }`) is refused differently --
"Cannot allocate unsized type": its type header was never written, because the payload slot's derivation found zero bytes past the
tag and answered "bad", in silence.

**The defect.** `ll_type` answers `void` for `NIL` -- right for a return position, where `Result<NIL>` is `{ i32 }` by a special
case, and wrong inside any aggregate -- and every aggregate lowering asked it: the struct body, the array element, the module
constant renderer; the struct literal inserted the sentinel's `i32 0` into the `void` slot, the assignment stored into it, the
constructor stored a `NIL` payload past a slot that `enum_payload_slot` had refused to derive because `sz - tag` was 0.

**The fix.** One mapping, `ll_member_type` (ir_types.npk): a `NIL` member is the zero-sized `{}` -- size 0, alignment 1, exactly
what D-084 promises -- and every other member type is `ll_type`'s; `ll_struct_body` and `ll_array` read it (a struct whose fields
are ALL `NIL` is `i8`, by the zero-field rule: two values need two addresses); `enum_payload_slot` admits zero bytes past the tag
(`[0 x iK]`, a legal zero-sized area). And nothing touches a `NIL` member: the struct literal inserts nothing into it
(`zeroinitializer` already holds it), a read of one is the sentinel's own `i32 0` (`emit_member_from`), a store into one lands
nowhere (`emit_assign`), the module-constant renderer writes `{} zeroinitializer` (`emit_global_struct_into`), and the enum
constructor stores no `NIL` payload (both the single and the several-payload paths) -- the value is evaluated for its effects in
each case. The binding of a `NIL` payload by name needed nothing (measured).

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/nil_field.npk`: the literal, two reads, a store into the `NIL` field, `#size_of<S>()` of
`{ int32; NIL; int32 }` answering 8, a module constant read, `Box<NIL>`, an array of such structs, a struct holding a string beside
the `NIL` field (its drop body), an enum with a `NIL` payload constructed and matched beside an `int32` payload bound -- exit 0 at
both legs; landing 111's compiler emits invalid IR for it. The six probes (`p232a`, `c`, `e`, `f`, `s`, `g`) exit 0. OPEN_DECISIONS
marks DEF-232 FIXED (the decision to read was D-084, not D-105); DECISIONS carries a dated note on D-084; TYPE_REFERENCE's `NIL`
bullet gains the lowering; the 1.6 README's Remaining list drops DEF-232.

**The measurements** (base = landing 111's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 111's checker and this one over 5,146 files (the
tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 0 sites vanished, 0
appeared -- 4,131 → 4,131: the checker accepted a `NIL` member before and after; the
landing is the emitter's. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 570 programs under both compilers, 569 byte-identical,
1 different -- the landing's own `nil_field.npk`, whose base emission is the invalid `{ i32, void, i32 }` -- 0
newly refused, 0 refused by both. The libraries and applications: 4,026 programs, 1,884 byte-identical,
2,139 refused by both, 3 different (the listener's F-048 probes `n1_nil_field.npk` and `n2_nil_field_built.npk` and the fuzzer's claim program `ty1944.npk` (D-084's "NIL is zero-sized"), each a struct with a `NIL` field whose base emission `llc` refused -- `type { i32, void }`, "void type only allowed for function results" -- and whose emission is `type { i32, {} }` now, the type line and the literal's skipped insert the whole difference), 0 newly refused. D-332's count over the five
rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The verify suite's problem text: 133 of 133 files
byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 387 programs
at both legs, 378 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design.
The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's 35 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at
this tree — 7,077 obligations (3,427 discharged, 2,735 open, 0 budget, 910 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker;
`nitpick.obligations` 6,780 rows before and 6,781 after, 6,683 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among
shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell; the 97 rows not shared are RE-KEYED -- a type-id move: the problem texts of rows in 26 functions of `ir_expr`, `ir_types`, `numeric`, `smt_encode` and `smt_writer` name a struct type by its interned id (`|npk.f.<TYPEID>.field|`, D-317), the emitter's edited bodies interning ahead of them, the same rows per function verdict for verdict (25 of the 26 functions' (kind, verdict) multisets equal both ways) -- and the one row added is `ll_struct_body`'s one `overflow` row, `open`: the count of the members it keeps while it skips a `NIL` field, so the (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets of the two manifests differ by exactly that one (0 removed); the floor's 388 unmoved. The
in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A LOUD WRONG-TOOL REFUSAL FIXED: a struct, an array or an enum with a `NIL` member compiles and runs
where `llc` refused its emission; no refusal moves; no emission moves for a program `llc` accepted before (the `{}` member appears
only where `void` did); no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in advance (NOTICES F54).

### DEF-235, DEF-236 (c), (d), (e), DEF-237 and DEF-234 (c) — LANDED 2026-10-15 (built by `nitpick-compiler_36`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_38`; worktree `wt/36a`, branch `36-small` on landing 112's commit; landing 113; the library listener's F-029, F-036 (a), F-039 (c)–(e), F-042 (a), F-045 and F-034 (c) in O-N35/O-N36)

**Where it started.** The listener's M11 session (reported 2026-10-07) left five lower-priority rows of the lexical, AST,
traits, diagnostic and module runs, each a program the reference refuses and the compiler accepted or misadvised, none a
wrong answer: a keyword declared as a name and then unusable (`func:acquire`, `wild int8->:buffer`), a `joins` deadline
that was a call, an attribute the language does not have, an `opaque struct` at module level, the `??`-on-`Result` advice
naming the retired `?`, and a constant cycle reported once per member with no member named. MEASURED FIRST on landing 110's
compiler (`wt/36a/.internal/probes113/`): `func:acquire`, `func:any`, `func:trit`, `func:nit`, `struct:any`, `mod:trit`,
a parameter `int32:acquire`, a `for` binding `int32:any`, `wild int8->:buffer` and `fixed int32:any` each compiled (exit
0) and could never be named; a METHOD `func:acquire` in an `impl` compiled and `x.acquire()` was TYPE-019 "no method" --
the exemption DEF-103 made for method names was dead; `opaque struct:OpHandle;` at module level compiled; `joins raw
duration_secs(1i64)` and `joins Duration{ ns: n }` (a parameter's value) compiled; `#[lexical_drop]` on a struct,
`#[nosuch]` on a function, an impl and a local compiled; `??` on a `Result` advised `?` (PARSE-011 when followed); a
`FIRST`/`SECOND` cycle was two RESOLVE-006, a three-way cycle three, none naming the chain. The listener's own scripts
(`lx0054`, `lx0114`, `lx0121`) failed their claims for exactly the four function names.

**The fixes.** (1) DEF-235: every declared name of every kind is an identifier -- `p_declared_name` (parse_decl.npk)
exempts no keyword (the four were let through for method names, and a keyword token in declaration position carries no
intern, so the method so declared was never found while a module-level function so named was accepted) and reads a
trait's, an `assoc`'s, an `opaque struct`'s, an error's, a unit's, a rule's and a macro's name too; `p_binding_name`
(parser.npk) reads a local's, a module binding's, a parameter's, a `for` binding's, a field's and a variant's name
(`wild int8->:buffer` took the keyword through the qualifier's early "a declaration" in `p_looks_like_decl`, where
`int64:buffer` was never a declaration). PARSE-001 naming the keyword, once; the dotted spellings `m.acquire()`, `v.any()`,
`t.trit(i)`, `n.nit(i)` are the compiler's own intercepts, declared nowhere. (2) DEF-236 (c): the `joins` deadline is a
constant by the module-constant gate's own verdict (`const_init_verdict`: an inline `Duration{ ns: … }` of constants, a
`fixed` module binding of `Duration`, `comptime(…)`), else `NITPICK-TYPE-045` at the clause -- D-083's "fixed where the
executor is created". (3) DEF-236 (d): an attribute whose name is not `derive` is `NITPICK-DERIVE-007` (new) wherever it
stands -- a struct, an enum, a trait, a function, an impl, a method (`attrs_known`, `attrs_in_members` in the derive
reader's walk, derive_gen.npk) -- and ANY attribute on a local binding is (`check_var_decl`, type_stmt.npk); `#[derive]`
on an impl is DERIVE-004's sentence; `#[lexical_drop]`/`#[nll_drop]` are named as D-003's removals. (4) DEF-236 (e):
`opaque struct:Name;` at module level, an inline module's too, is `NITPICK-RESOLVE-014` (new) at the declaration in the
loader's owned-name walk (`owned_names_in_module`, module_graph.npk); inside an `extern` block it stays EXTERN-001's
reserved tier (D-190). (5) DEF-237: the advice reads "a `Result` is unwrapped by `?!` (trap on the error) or `?|` (a
fallback value), D-175 -- the bare `?` is retired". (6) DEF-234 (c): a constant cycle is ONE RESOLVE-006 at its first
member in source order, naming the members in the order they refer to each other ("`A` reads `B` reads `C` reads `A`";
`init_cycle_chain`, `init_mentions`, resolve.npk -- the shape walk `init_reaches` makes, collecting the module bindings an
initialiser reads; a member whose chain passes through an earlier binding is not reported again, D-240). FOUND BUILDING IT,
by the new parser refusing the compiler's own source: FOUR KEYWORD-NAMED FIELDS in the tree -- `FloatScan.end` (numeric.npk),
`FInstr.atomic` (npkg/floor_instr.npk), the encoder's `result` term and `Counter.limit` (smt_encode.npk) -- each "working"
only because a keyword token carries no intern on either side, so the declaration's name 0 matched the member read's name 0
(a second such field in one struct would have aliased the first in silence; each struct had exactly one); renamed
`tail_end`, `is_atomic`, `res_term`, `bound`; `tests/expansion/rejection/extern_shape.npk`'s `opaque struct:Handle` (a
type keyword) is `Hnd`, `vacant_init.npk`'s field `OwnedFd:fd` is `desc` and `parse_decls.npk`'s `macro:pure` is
`decls_only` -- a pre-sweep of the tree's 1,064 files with the new checker found no sixth. And the first form of (4) named
a local `on` -- a keyword -- and the compiler refused the compiler: DEF-235's own lesson, twice.

### DEF-250 — LANDED 2026-10-15 (built by `nitpick-compiler_37`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_38`; worktree `wt/37a`, branch `37-def250` on landing 113's commit; landing 114; the library seats' report of 2026-10-12)

**Where it started.** The library seat (nitpick-libs_19, 2026-10-12) relayed the Team seat's nitpick-time 0.3.3 plan, whose
§1.2 had measured a shape the reference refuses and the compiler accepted: `int64:counter;` at module level passes the checker
-- and so do a bare struct, a `stack`, `wild`, `wildx` or `pub` binding, an `atomic<int64>` and a channel -- where
`int64:counter = 0i64;` is NITPICK-TYPE-055 (D-211: module state is `fixed`, and nothing else). Found at `7e91730`, reproduced
at `e50eaf4` and registered as DEF-250 by `nitpick-compiler_37` on 2026-10-15 (the row rode landing 113). MEASURED FIRST on
landing 111's compiler: the seat's `bare_binding.npk` (`counter = 5i64; if (counter != 5i64) { exit 11i32; } exit 0i32;`)
compiles and exits 107 at -O0 -- `MachineFault`, a store into read-only memory, since the binding lowers to `@"npk.<module>.counter"
= constant i64 zeroinitializer` -- and 11 at -O2, where `opt` deletes the store into a constant as undefined and folds the read to
the constant's zero: the two legs disagree (DEF-106's shape), and the -O2 answer is a wrong VALUE from a program the checker
accepted. A `fixed` binding with no initialiser was refused already (ASSIGN-001, the plan's measurement).

**The defect.** The D-211 check in `check_decl`'s global arm (type_stmt.npk) sat inside the initialiser's branch (`if (!(raw
expr_is_none(init)))`), so a binding with no initialiser was asked nothing; the rule was "every module binding is `fixed`" and the
check was asked of every module binding WITH an initialiser.

**The fix.** The check is hoisted out of the branch and asked of every module binding, initialiser or not: a plain binding of any
type with no initialiser is NITPICK-TYPE-055 at the declaration, once (the writes and the reads that follow add nothing: D-240);
`fixed` with a compile-time constant initialiser stays the one spelling of module state (D-211). The refusal's sentence is the one
it always was.

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/global_mutable.npk` gains four no-initialiser sites -- a bare `int64` a function writes, a bare
`pub int32`, a bare struct `Pair`, a bare `atomic<int64>` -- one `expect-error` line per site (D-332's count: seven TYPE-055 sites,
seven lines, where landing 113's checker reports three); the seat's `bare_binding.npk` is refused at `3:1`, one report. DECISIONS
carries a dated note on D-211; TYPE_REFERENCE's module-binding paragraph says the keyword is required with or without an
initialiser; OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-250 FIXED; the 1.6 README's Remaining list drops it.

**The measurements** (base = landing 113's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 113's checker and this one over 5,157 files (the
tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 4 sites appeared (the four TYPE-055 of `global_mutable.npk`'s no-initialiser sites -- a bare `int64` a function writes, a bare `pub int32`, a bare struct, a bare `atomic<int64>`), 0
vanished -- 4,217 → 4,221; per code NITPICK-TYPE-055 5 -> 9. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 570 programs under both
compilers, 570 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 0 refused by
both. The libraries and applications: 4,033 programs, 1,819 byte-identical, 2,214 refused by both, 0
different, 0 newly refused. D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched
files. The verify suite's problem text: 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133
files PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 387 programs at both legs, 378 at their expected exit, the nine
`// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's
35 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree — 7,097 obligations (3,437 discharged, 2,740 open, 0 budget, 915 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler
rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; `nitpick.obligations` 6,799 rows before and 6,799
after, 6,799 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts
fell; no row re-keyed, added or removed -- the two manifests hold the same 6,799 rows and their (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets are EQUAL: the hoisted check in `check_decl` interns no type ahead of any row and carries no row of its own; the floor's 388 unmoved. The in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** A REFUSAL ADDED: a module binding with no initialiser is TYPE-055 where it compiled to a read-only
constant that faulted at -O0 and read back zero at -O2 -- a silent wrong answer at -O2 moved to the checker; no library or application program is refused anew;
no emission moves for a program that compiles on both; no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. Announced in
advance (NOTICES F56).

### DEF-251 (i) — LANDED 2026-10-15 (built by `nitpick-compiler_37`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_38`; worktree `wt/37b`, branch `37-def251` on landing 114's commit; landing 115; the library seats' report of 2026-10-12)

**Where it started.** The library seat's report of 2026-10-12 (nitpick-libs_19, beside DEF-250's shape): `int64->:p = NULL;
<-p = 5i64;` in `main` passes the checker -- TYPE_REFERENCE §27 says `NULL` is valid in a pointer context, and no rule checked
a dereference -- and the two legs disagreed. At -O0 the hardware fault entered the trap route as `MachineFault` (exit 107,
D-307's last net). At -O2 `opt` read a store to `null` as undefined behaviour and lowered `main` to `entry: unreachable` -- an
EMPTY body -- so the processor ran into whatever function the linker placed next: `npk_failsafe` with a garbage argument (its
`(*)` arm, exit 99), or a `never fails` helper declared between `main` and `failsafe` (exit 0: the program REPORTED SUCCESS).
An uncontrolled path in safe code at -O2, the class the language exists to prevent; found at `7e91730`, reproduced at
`e50eaf4`, registered as DEF-251 by `nitpick-compiler_37` on 2026-10-15 (the row rode landing 113) with two halves: (i) the legs
must agree, mechanically; (ii) whether a plain pointer may hold `NULL` at all -- S-136, the user's, settled the same morning as
D-355 (`NULL` is legal only for a `wild` pointer, "maybe no pointer" is `T->?` with `NIL`, a plain `T->` is never null:
planned first, its own landing). MEASURED FIRST on landing 111's compiler: `null_store`, `null_store_helper` and
`fixed_null_store` (a module-level `fixed int64->:P = NULL;` written through) exit 107 at -O0 and 99, 0 and 99 at -O2.

**The defect.** Nothing in the emission told LLVM that address zero is a memory location: under LLVM's default a load from or a
store to `null` is undefined behaviour, so the optimiser deleted the store, inferred the rest of the body unreachable and emitted
no code after it -- the fall-through into the next function is what an empty body does on this machine.

**The fix.** Every emitted `define` carries LLVM's `null_pointer_is_valid` function attribute beside `"split-stack"` -- one text,
`ll_fn_open` (ir_types.npk) -- under which a store to or a load from `null` is a memory operation like any other and nothing is
inferred from a dereference: the -O2 leg faults as the -O0 leg does, the fault is the trap route's, and a later `p == NULL` test
is kept. The FIRST form, the quoted string `"null-pointer-is-valid"`, is an attribute no pass reads (measured: it changed nothing
at -O2); LLVM's keyword is the unquoted `null_pointer_is_valid`. The floor's functions carry neither attribute (they never did).
The cost: the folding LLVM no longer does -- a sample of eight programs' `opt -O2` + `llc -O2` objects gave 7 of 8 byte-identical
in size and `dyn_slots` 6,928 → 6,992 bytes (nine `null` compares of the `dyn` cell kept where -O2 used to fold them).

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/null_store_legs.npk` (`// expect-exit: 107`): the store through `NULL` with a `never
fails` helper declared between `main` and `failsafe` on purpose -- the base exits 107 at -O0 and 0 at -O2 (the empty `main` fell
into `helper`), this compiler 107 at both; the harness runs every program at both legs and requires one exit. The three probes
exit 107 at both legs. The emitter's three unit tests (`tests/backend/ir_expr.npk`, `ir_func.npk`, `ir_stmt.npk`) compare whole functions' text
exactly, so their nineteen literal `define` lines (8, 6 and 5) expect the attribute now -- the chain's backend step found all three
red under the first form of this tree (exit 11, 11 and 10), where the hand-off's note had said they spell no opener, a grep whose
recursive search is broken on this machine; the emission comparison is read with the attribute stripped (`meta/roadmap/1.6/tools/attrnorm.py`, the twin of
`tidnorm.py` and `sitenorm.py`; the libraries' `emit_one.sh` strips before its byte compare). DECISIONS carries a dated note on
D-307; TYPE_REFERENCE §27's `NULL` section gains the bullet; OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-251 (i) FIXED and (ii) D-355's; the 1.6
README's Remaining list drops DEF-251.

**The measurements** (base = landing 114's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 114's checker and this one over 5,158 files (the
tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 0 sites vanished, 0
appeared -- 4,221 → 4,221: the checker is untouched; the landing is the emitter's. THE
EMISSION COMPARISON: 571 programs under both compilers, 0 byte-identical, 571 different in TEXT -- every
program compiling under both, by the attribute on each of its defines (17,831 attributes stripped) -- and 571 of
571 IDENTICAL once `attrnorm.py` strips it, 0 different beyond it; 0 newly refused,
0 refused by both. The libraries and applications: 4,033 programs, 1,819 identical with the attribute
stripped, 2,214 refused by both, 0 different beyond it, 0 newly refused. D-332's count
over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The verify suite's problem text: 133 of 133 files
byte-identical under both compilers; vprog: 133 files PASS, 0 FAIL. The program suite by hand: 388 programs
at both legs, 379 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design.
The frontend's and the emitter's unit tests: the frontend's 35 and the emitter's 5 pass. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at
this tree — 7,097 obligations (3,437 discharged, 2,740 open, 0 budget, 915 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker;
`nitpick.obligations` 6,799 rows before and 6,799 after, 6,799 shared, ZERO verdicts moved among
shared rows, ZERO (symbol, kind) discharged counts fell; no row re-keyed, added or removed -- the two manifests hold the same 6,799 rows and their (symbol, kind, verdict) multisets are EQUAL: a row hashes its problem text and never the emitted IR, so the attribute `ll_fn_open` writes on every define reaches no row, and the edit interns no type ahead of any row and carries no row of its own; the floor's 388 unmoved. The
in-process checks 0 failures.

**What changes for a program.** AN UNCONTROLLED PATH CLOSED: a store through or a load from a `NULL` pointer reaches `failsafe` as
`MachineFault` at both legs where the -O2 leg fell through into the next function; no refusal moves; EVERY emission moves in text
by one attribute per `define` and in nothing else; the object moves only where LLVM had folded a `null` assumption (the sample's
one program, +64 bytes); no computed answer; no floor byte; no snapshot refresh. THE LIBRARIES RE-PIN ONCE AT LANDING 118 -- one re-pin carrying landings 104 through 118, the author's settlement of 2026-10-15
12:04 relayed by the library seat at 113's verification; an adoption round that starts before 118 lands takes the newest landing on
main then, never one before this -- as F57 says. Announced in advance (NOTICES F57).

### DEF-252 — LANDED 2026-10-15 (`nitpick-compiler_38`; worktree `wt/38a`, branch `38-def252` on landing 115's commit; landing 116; the library seat's report of 2026-10-15 09:20)

**Where it started.** The library seat (nitpick-libs_19, 2026-10-15 09:20) relayed a finding of nitpick-parse's cycle-0.0
probes -- found by the Team seat's planner, reproduced by Ailp-team_1 and by the library seat at `7e91730`, reproduced here
on landing 113's compiler at 09:21: `struct:Cursor = { fixed uint8[]:src; int64:pos; }; func:reset = NIL(Cursor->:c, fixed
uint8[]:src) never fails { <-c = Cursor{ src: src, pos: 0i64 }; pass NIL; };` called `drop reset(@c, string_bytes(t));` inside a
block that declares the string `t`, with `c` declared outside it. `npkc` exits 0 and the program exits 170 at -O0 and at -O2 --
0xAA, the allocator's free poison: `c.src` reads `t`'s freed heap body after the block. A use-after-free in safe code, at both
legs, the class the author treats as blocking; `management_0` placed it right behind landings 114 and 115 at 09:20. The same
re-seat written as a DIRECT assignment in the block (`c = Cursor{ src: string_bytes(t), pos: 0i64 };`) is `NITPICK-BORROW-002`
(D-004 rule 3 as DEF-42 reads it, 1.5.5 step 0): the holder outlives the storage the view names. Registered as DEF-252 the same
morning (the row rode landing 113) with the reading a read-only groundwork job (Opus, from this seat's prompt through
`management_0`'s credit offer, $7.55) confirmed line by line and extended by two reach items.

**The defect.** DEF-42's question -- is the holder's binding declared outside the block that declares the borrowed local? -- was
asked in `escape_assign`'s bare-local branch alone, at a direct store (`check_holder_scope`, escape.npk). A callee's store is
connected by D-325's provenance summaries: `reset`'s store matrix says its view parameter flows into its pointer parameter's
pointee, `record_dest_refs` collects the refs the arguments carry and `refuse_view_escape_into` decides by the holder's KIND --
a pointer parameter's pointee and a lent `dyn`'s cell refuse (BORROW-002), a by-value parameter and a local HOLD (`escape_mark`,
so a later `pass c` is BORROW-001) -- and never by its scope. Two gaps beside it, the groundwork's: a by-value `move` parameter
holder outlives an inner block's string just the same, and a destination behind a dereferenced pointer (`reset(@(<-p), …)`) has no
root identifier, so the call path returned in silence where the direct `(<-p) = …` of a frame view is rule 3's generic refusal.

**The fix.** `record_dest_refs` hands `refuse_view_escape_into` the arguments whose refs it flowed into the destination (`srcs`),
and the holder branches ask the question of each: `holder_scope_violated` (the predicate factored out of `check_holder_scope`,
asked by both -- `collect_local_roots` over the argument's own text, exactly as the direct assignment asks it of its value) and
`refuse_call_holder_scope` (BORROW-002 at the destination argument, one report per destination, naming the rule: "a view of a
local declared inside a block is stored by this call into a binding declared outside that block"). A LOCAL holder is bound by its
own scope; a BY-VALUE PARAMETER holder by the body's block (the resolver declares a parameter in the FUNCTION scope and opens the
body one scope below, while the parameter's storage and the body's own locals die together with the frame -- the first form bound
the parameter by its function scope and refused `dyn_dest.npk`'s legal `move dyn` and `move Stash` holders and its `fill_drop`
control, read in the three-suite comparison before the chain); a destination with no root identifier -- `@(<-p)` -- is refused as
the direct store is. A `dyn` or bound call is a KNOWN callee (the union of its impls' summaries, D-325), so the method receiver,
the `dyn` and the function value reach the same branch by their own roads.

**The tests.** `tests/analysis/rejection/view_reseat_call.npk`: sixteen BORROW-002 sites, one per shape -- the probe's
whole-pointee store, a field store (the grouped view type `(fixed uint8[]):src`, since a `fixed` field is write-once), the store
two calls down, the prelude's `list_push` and `list_insert` of a view into a list declared outside the view's block, a method's
implicit receiver, a `dyn`'s method, a function value (an unknown callee stores every argument into every pointee), a view inside a
struct argument, an awaited async callee, an ADDRESS stored through a callee (DEF-42's own shape, done by the callee), a loop

reports it too) and a view-holding local of the inner block -- and seven controls carrying no code: the same block, the holder
declared inside the view's block, a callee that reads the view and stores a scalar, a callee that stores a copy, a `move`
parameter holder with the string in the body's own block, a literal's view, a parameter-rooted view. Its header is generated to a

**Registered with it.** DEF-256 — the fuzzer's F-049 (nitpick-fuzz_2's report of 2026-10-15 20:35, nitpick-libs_19 copied;
nitpick-fuzz main `f31dfc6`, `findings/F-049-pick-binding-reinit-refused/`; reproduced here at 20:42 on landing 115's compiler and

### D-353's refusal (DEF-236 (a)) — LANDED 2026-10-16 (built by `nitpick-compiler_37`, landed by `nitpick-compiler_38`; worktree `wt/37c`, branch `37-def236a` on landing 116's commit; landing 117; the library listener's F-039 (a) in O-N36, the fuzzer's `as0062`)

**Where it started.** AST_REFERENCE §4's `GenericParam` row promised a compile-time VALUE parameter -- `func:scale<comptime
int32:K> = int32(int32:x) never fails { pass (x * K); }` called `scale::<3i32>(2i32)` -- and the checker accepted the form while
the emitter, monomorphising by TYPE argument alone (D-191's instance key), stopped on the call with EMIT-002: the wrong tool, no
sentence (the listener's F-039 (a), 2026-10-07; the fuzzer's `as0062`, "expect: run:0"). S-135 put the language question to the
user -- implement value generics, or refuse them by name until designed -- with the library side's input relayed verbatim (no
planned library needs user-declared value parameters; the language already gives them to one builtin, `Channel<T, LEVEL, CAP>`),
and the user settled it on 2026-10-15 as D-353 ("all the recommendations look fine to me. Lets go with those."), with D-354 beside
it: the form stays RESERVED under a code of its own, so D-336's frozen corpus can record a verdict that may move from refused to
accepted after the freeze and never back.

**The fix.** `NITPICK-TYPE-095` at the parameter, once, whatever declares it -- a function, a struct, a trait, an enum, an impl --
in the bounds walk every declaration's generic window goes through (`collect_param_bounds`, type_trait.npk: the one pass the
pipeline and the unit test's helper both run; the first form sat in `check_decl`, which `type_generic.npk`'s helper never

value arguments as not this form. The USES say nothing more (D-240): the resolver's kind mismatch, not-constant and suffix
mismatch reports at a value argument are silenced behind the declaration's one report, and a value argument that folds interns as
before. `Mutex`'s level message loses its article ("`Mutex`'s lock level is a constant, not a type" where it read "the lock level").

int32:LEVEL>` struct, and its two use-rule cases -- a type where a value was wanted, a non-constant -- are re-spelled on the
compiler-known `Mutex<Point, int32>` and `Mutex<Point, (n)>` (TYPE-016 each, D-072's gate, under landing 116's checker as under this one: the sweep moves nothing in the file). `tests/frontend/type_generic.npk`'s
value-parameter cases are re-pointed by measurement (one TYPE-095 each; two keep their body's own second report; the helper's
limitation noted in the file). AST_REFERENCE §4 and TRAITS_REFERENCE §3.1.1 say RESERVED (D-353) with the day's spellings;
OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-236 (a) FIXED; DECISIONS' D-353 carries the landing note; the 1.6 README's Remaining list drops the row.

### DEF-253 and DEF-254 — LANDED 2026-10-16 (`nitpick-compiler_38`; worktree `wt/38b`, branch `38-def253` on landing 117's commit; landing 118; the library seat's report of 2026-10-15 09:20)

**Where it started.** The library seat (nitpick-libs_19, 2026-10-15 09:20, beside DEF-252's finding) relayed nitpick-parse's
probe06: `trait:Src = { func:step = int32(Self->:self) never fails; }; impl:Count:Src = { … }; func:drive<F: Src> = int32(F->:f)
never fails { pass (raw f.step()); };` called `drive::<Count>(@c)` -- the static plugin path nitpick-parse's 0.2.2 is written
around (PD-131: every method takes `Self->`, driven through a bound). `npkc` exits 0 and `llc` refuses the emission: "use of
undefined value '@npk.probe06_trait_bound.Count->:Src.step'". The by-value bound (`F:f`, `Self:self`) and the concrete pointer
call (`c.step()` on a `Count->`) both run. LOUD: the wrong tool refuses a program the language admits; registered as DEF-253
the same morning (the row rode landing 113), the reading confirmed by a read-only groundwork job (Opus, $4.21, through
`management_0`'s credit offer) that named the one line and flagged a neighbour it could not verify by reading.

**The defects.** D-156 qualifies a method's symbol by the impl's module and TARGET: the definition mints `Count:Src.step` from the
impl's resolved target (`impl_method_sym`), and the checker records the same from the peeled receiver when an impl answers. A call
through a BOUND records no symbol (at check time the receiver is the parameter `F`, and there is no impl yet), so the emitter's sync
bound-call branch (`emit_method_call`, ir_expr.npk, 1.0.6b) rebuilds the name from the SUBSTITUTED receiver -- and built it from the
receiver as written, `Count->`, peeling the pointer only afterwards, for `impl_decl_for`; the async twin (`async_callee_name`) had
peeled first since 1.1.12c. Beside it, DEF-254, verified by this landing's test on its first run: inside a trait DEFAULT whose
receiver is `Self->`, `self.tick()` types the receiver as the POINTER to `Self`, the `Self`-receiver test in the same branch (and in
the async twin) read the pointer's kind and missed, and the call named the bare `tick` -- a symbol nothing defines, on every
compiler since D-185 admitted `Self->` receivers, through a bound or not; an `async` default awaiting a sibling method on `self`
had the same miss by construction (an async receiver is always a pointer). And a family impl answering through `F->` recorded no
instance (`type_decl_of` answers 0 for a pointer).

**The fix.** The sync bound-call branch peels the one pointer level BEFORE it builds the name and notes the family instance (as `.`
always peels, D-098), and both branches peel `Self->` before the `Self`-receiver test. Two readers of a receiver's type, one rule.

**The tests.** `tests/backend/programs/bound_ptr_receiver.npk`, eight shapes checked once each, exit 0 at both legs: the row's shape
with the turbofish and inferred; probe06's (a failable method, two impls through one bound, `relay`, a `pick` over the stepped
value); the by-value bound and the concrete pointer call as controls; a family impl `impl:<T>:Box<T>:Src` driven through `F->` (the
instance noted from the peeled type: `Box<int32>:Src.step` is emitted); a trait default through `F->`; a two-trait bound
(`F: Src & Val`); a field receiver of pointer-to-parameter type (`Holder<F>->` with `F->:f`); and DEF-254's own shape, the default
called on a concrete pointer. Landing 117's compiler emits `Box<int32>->:Src.step` and the bare `tick` for it, and `llc` refuses the
module. `tests/backend/programs/generic_list.npk`'s comment, which said a trait method cannot take `Self->`, is corrected (dated).
OPEN_DECISIONS marks DEF-253 FIXED and DEF-254 FIXED with it; DECISIONS carries a dated note on D-156 (the `<Target>` half is the
impl's target, a pointer receiver's pointee, at every end); TRAITS_REFERENCE §5.1 says what a call through a bound names. **DEF-255
REGISTERED OPEN** by the probe the groundwork asked for: the QUALIFIED call `Src.step(f)` through a bound, `Src.val(f)` by value
through a bound and `Src.step(c)` on a concrete pointer are each TYPE-019 where TRAITS_REFERENCE's D-172 paragraph promises the
bound form (`type_trait_qualified_call` searches the impl table for the unpeeled receiver alone); only the concrete by-value form
compiles. Low priority: no library spells it.

**The measurements** (base = landing 117's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 117's checker and this one over 5,162 files (the
tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 0 sites vanished, 0

### D-355 — LANDED 2026-10-16 (`nitpick-compiler_38`; worktree `wt/38c`, branch `38-d355` on landing 118's commit; landing 119; the user's ruling of 2026-10-15 through `management_0`, S-136 option (c); planned first at `.internal/handoff_35/D355_PLAN.md`)

**Where it started.** The library seats' report of 2026-10-12 (DEF-251): `int64->:p = NULL; <-p = 5i64;` compiled -- TYPE_REFERENCE
§27 admitted `NULL` in any pointer context -- and the legs disagreed, a fault at -O0 and a fall-through at -O2. Landing 115 made
both legs fault (DEF-251 (i)); whether a plain pointer may hold `NULL` at all was S-136, settled by the user on 2026-10-15 as
D-355: a plain `T->` always points at something, `NULL` keeps its meaning in a `wild` pointer context alone, and "maybe no
pointer" is the Optional `T->?` tested against `NIL` -- "planned first, its own landing".

**What the plan measured** (2026-10-16, on landing 118's compiler, fourteen probes; `D355_PLAN.md` §0). The Optional pointer works
end to end today: `int64->?:p = NIL; p = @x; p != NIL; p ?? @x; p?.v` (a field of the pointee, still wrapped), a `T->?` parameter,
return and struct field, lowered as `{ i8, ptr }`, the escape analysis seeing a borrow inside it as it sees the bare pointer's
(`pass p` of an `int64->?` holding `@x` is BORROW-001). Its limit: `?.` reaches a FIELD only (`p?.get()` is TYPE-019), so a `Self->`
method on the pointee is called on `p ?? @d` and the no-default form was S-137's question, asked of the user with this landing's plan and SETTLED the same morning as D-360 (`??!`, the Optional twin of `?!`: landing 120, its own F-notice).
TWO HOLES the ruling had not named: `int64->?:p = NULL;` compiled and built a PRESENT optional holding a null pointer
(`expected_value` unwraps the slot to the pointer and the sentinel arm admitted it), and `fixed int64->:P = NULL;` compiled
(`fixed wild int64->:P = NULL;` is the module-level spelling that stays). THE CENSUS CORRECTED: the compiler's own 22 `NULL`
sites are not plain pointers "which become `T->?`" -- they are the emitter's context struct's four `wild` fields (`LlCtx.mod_of`,
`.tr`, `.obl`, `.impls`) and `assoc_binding_of`'s `wild ImplTable->:t`, legal under the ruling's own words, so nothing in `src/`
changes and the context pointers stay `wild` (re-spelling them `T->?` would need S-137's operator at 236 reads for no
behavioural change); `lib/` and `npkg/` hold 9 `wild` buffer resets, legal; the tree's tests held 107 non-`wild` sites in 10
files; the six libraries and the applications 0; the fuzz corpus 15 lines in 12 programs. And one neighbour: a `pick` with a
`(NULL)` arm over a `wild` pointer selector is EMIT-002 on every compiler (DEF-257, registered OPEN, low).

**The rule.** `NITPICK-TYPE-096` (`TYPE_NULL_PLAIN_POINTER`), reported ONCE at the literal by the checker's sentinel arm
(`type_sentinel`, type_expr.npk) wherever a pointer or an `Optional` is expected and the slot is not a `wild` pointer context;
three faces of one sentence (the plain slot; an `Optional` slot: "write `NIL`"; a comparison: "`p == NULL` is a question a plain
pointer always answers `false`"); where no pointer is expected at all, TYPE-UNKNOWN's "nothing here says which pointer" stands.
A `wild` pointer context is one the slot's DECLARATION names, or a place of wild provenance by D-120's one predicate
(`wild_places.npk`): a `wild`/`wildx` local's or module binding's initialiser, a store into a wild place (a `wild` binding,
parameter or field, an element of one), a `wild` parameter's argument (a user function's by `param_quals`, a builtin's by the
signature table's `wild any->` text: `dalloc(NULL)` and `ralloc(NULL, n)` stay legal), a `wild` field in a struct literal, a `pass`
from a `wild`-returning function (`DECL_RET_WILD`, and the `Result{ value: … }` literal's value), an `=>! wild T->` cast's operand,
and either operand of `==`/`!=` whose other operand has wild provenance. THE MECHANISM: `ExprTyper.null_ctx`, a context word
beside `expected` -- 0 a plain slot, 1 a wild one, 2 a comparison against a plain pointer -- set by the declared slots (save,
set, type, restore: `expected`'s discipline) and RESET to 0 by `type_of_expr` at every node that is not transparent
(`null_ctx_passes`: the sentinel, a ternary, a `??`/`?|` default, a pick expression, an array literal), so a wild slot's context
reaches a `NULL` through a ternary's arm, a default, a `give` or an element and never into a nested call's argument, a literal's
field or a cast's operand, which set their own (probed: `wild int8->:w = raw f(NULL)` with `f` plain is refused at the argument,
`int8->:p = is (c) : NULL : @z` at the arm). D-240: a sealed header write `s.ptr = NULL` keeps its one TYPE-079 (measured), and
`discard(NULL)`, `int32:x = NULL` their existing reports. `place_wild` reads a `wild` MODULE binding and `wildx` as it read a
`wild` local, parameter and `=>! wildx` cast (the plan's probe found `fixed wild int64->:P = NULL;` admitted and `P != NULL`
refused), and `expr_wild_provenance` reads the compiler-known headers' `ptr` (a `string`'s, `cstring`'s, slice's or `buffer`'s)
as a wild place -- the allocator's block pointer, null for an empty body by the floor's own representation (TYPE_REFERENCE §23's
"null ptr, `cap == 0`"), the shape the prelude's `List` declares as `hidden wild T->:items` -- so `b.ptr != NULL` stands (the
chain's first run refused the fuzz corpus's `ty1755` there; read, and the arm added before the chain ran again). No emitter, encoder, REACH or floor change: `ptr null` stays the wild sentinel's lowering, the Optional's `NIL` its
`zeroinitializer`.

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/null_plain_pointer.npk`: twelve sites, one per shape -- a module binding, `pass NULL` from a
plain `T->()`, a plain local's initialiser, an assignment to it, a plain field in a literal, an assignment to the field, a plain
parameter's argument, `p == NULL`, `NULL != h.inner`, an `Optional` slot, an array literal's element, a `??` default into a plain

`null_store_legs.npk` stores through a `wild` null pointer (107 at both legs, re-measured); `tests/frontend/type_access.npk` keeps

libraries or the applications could have held it. The library seat's three observations of the day were measured beside it
(`probes120/kw*.npk`) and registered (DEF-258…DEF-260, below).

**The change.** ONE TOKEN: `"??!"` joins LEXICAL_REFERENCE §5's `Operator` production and the generator's name table
(`OpQuestionQuestionBang`), so the three generated tables carry it -- `token_kind.npk` (261 kinds; the kinds after it renumbered,
which nothing reads), `operators.npk` (the three-character block: longest match, `??!` ahead of `??` and `?!`), `token_name.npk`
-- and §5.3's trailing-`!` table names it (D-046). ONE NODE: `EmphaticCoalesceExpr` in AST_REFERENCE §3.4, which the generator
reads into `ast_kind.npk` (49 expression kinds), shape TWO in `ast.npk`'s table -- so every shape-driven walker (the clone, the
blanking, `ast_depth`, the escape, alias, bindings, exhaust and suspend walks) reaches it with no arm of its own -- and an arm in
each kind-keyed one: the parser's `p_coalesce` maps the token at `??`'s level; the typer's dispatch hands it to
`type_emphatic_coalesce` (type_expr.npk) -- the left operand an `Optional` (a `Result` is pointed at `?!`/`?|`, a plain pointer
at `T->?`: "a plain `T->` always points at something", D-355; anything else TYPE-007 by name), the right operand ONE `Error`
identity typed under `t_error` BEFORE the left is judged, as `?!`'s is (S-3b's lesson: a refused left still types the identity),
the result the inner type (a `T->?` yields its `T->`); the contract rules list it among the outcomes (TYPE-060, beside `?!`,

### D-361 — LANDED 2026-10-16 (`nitpick-compiler_39`; worktree `wt/39b`, branch `39-d361` on landing 120's commit; landing 121; the user's two rulings of 2026-10-16 through `management_0`: the `#ptr_add` confinement at 07:42:02 -- "I am fine with the fix you mentioned for the safety gap." -- and S-138 option (a) at 07:54:24 -- "i am fine with option A. If we find b is neccessary later we can always go with that." -- and his approval of this landing's form at 13:26:57 through `management_1`: "aprove the #ptr_add form")

**Where it started.** D-341's groundwork (2026-10-15) found `#ptr_add`'s result a plain `T->` with no context rule, where
BUILTIN_REFERENCE:425 says "legal only in `wild` context": `int32->:q = #ptr_add<int32>(p, 3i64); <-q` compiled in safe code
(the fuzzer's F-028 under DEF-154). `management_0` asked whether D-355 covered it; it did not -- the result is wild by
PROVENANCE (D-120's walk) and plain by TYPE, and `wild` is a place qualifier, no part of the pointer type. The user approved the
confinement at 07:42:02, and the same morning the wider seam behind it (`int32->:q = w;` from a `wild` binding, a wild value to
a plain parameter, an `=>! wild` cast's result into a plain binding -- `wild_copy.npk`, four roads compiling on 118's and 119's
compilers) was S-138, settled as D-361 (a): a value of wild provenance enters a plain pointer slot only through the explicit
crossing `v =>! T->`, measured over the three trees first, the compiler's own copies each given their cast, the roads covered
and not covered listed for the fuzzer's family.

**The reading.** The confinement IS D-361's rule at one producer: a `#ptr_add` result is of wild provenance, so it enters a
plain pointer slot (a binding, a parameter, a field, a return) only through `=>! T->`; a result DEREFERENCED IN PLACE --
`<-(#ptr_add<T>(p, k))`, TYPE_REFERENCE §23's own example and the Bridge's idiom at some sixty lines of `lib/nbridge.npk` --
stays legal, as every other wild-provenance producer's does: the in-place dereference IS the manual regime's read, and a named
`wild` binding before it would be the same operation spelled twice, churn without safety. `nitpick-compiler_38`'s provisional
note had the stricter form (the call accepted only into a wild slot, §23's example re-spelled); `management_1` agreed with this
form on the merits at 12:45:24 and put it to the author beside the stricter one; the author approved this form at 13:26:57
through `management_1`: "aprove the #ptr_add form".

**Measured first** (on landing 120's compiler): `wild_copy.npk`'s four roads compile; `wild_roads.npk`'s ten (a `pass` from a
plain `T->()`, a binding's initialiser from a wild binding, from `#ptr_add`, from an `=>! wild` cast, through a ternary's arm,
into an `Optional` slot, an assignment, a struct literal's field, a field assignment, a plain parameter's argument) compile.
The textual census: `#ptr_add` at 169 lines of the tree (lib/nbridge.npk 48, the generated Bridge stubs 9, lib/nfs.npk 8, the
rest tests) and 38 of the libraries (nitpick-fuzz 7 files, nitpick-time 4, nitpick-regex 1), nearly every one dereferenced in
place or handed to a `wild` slot. THE CHECKER'S CENSUS (the first form's checker beside 120's over the three trees, 5,169 files):
66 sites, every one TYPE-097 -- the compiler's own TWELVE, all a wild table handle (`LlCtx`'s `tr`/`obl`/`impls` fields, a

**The rule.** `NITPICK-TYPE-097` (`TYPE_WILD_INTO_PLAIN`), reported ONCE at the value by `check_wild_into_plain`
(type_expr.npk), asked right after a value is typed at the eight slots that set landing 119's `null_ctx` -- a local's and a

`pass` and a `Result{ value: … }`, a user call's and a builtin's argument (a function value's and a prelude generic's such as
`list_push`'s included), a struct literal's field (a generic instance's included) -- whenever the slot's type is a plain pointer
or an `Optional` of one, the slot is not a wild context (`null_ctx` 0, still the slot's when the check is asked) and the value
has wild provenance by D-120's one walk (`expr_wild_provenance`, depth 16); a ternary's arms, a `??`/`?|` default and a fixed
array literal's elements are looked into (`wild_into_plain_at`); the sentinel is excluded (TYPE-096's, D-240); a `#ptr_add`
result's sentence names the builtin. The crossing `v =>! T->` is the author's assertion that the pointer points at something a
program may read (the TOS marker, as every `=>!` is; `expr_wild_provenance` answers `false` for a plain-target cast); a `wild`
slot takes the value as it is. ROADS NOT COVERED, for the fuzzer's family: a pick expression's `give`, and a method call's
receiver (`c.get()` on a `wild Cell->` hands the body a plain `Self->`). No emitter, encoder, REACH or floor change: the cast
between one type id lowers to nothing.

### D-357 — LANDED 2026-10-16 (`nitpick-compiler_39`; worktree `wt/39c`, branch `39-d357` on landing 121's commit; landing 122; the user's ruling of 2026-10-15 at 07:50:47 through `management_0`, S-128 R1: "yes, pass those to compiler_37 too")

**Where it started.** `nitpick-compiler_28` wrote D-338's twin tests with one function called at both times and found DEF-214 (2026-10-01): the emitter has emitted no `comptime func:` since 1.0.9c, and the checker admitted a call of one anywhere a call may stand, so the call reached `llc` as an undefined symbol -- refused by the wrong tool with no sentence -- and inside an arithmetic node the same call was folded by the emitter and the program RAN. S-128 asked whether a `comptime func:` is also a run-time function; the recommendation was no, and the user ruled so as D-357.

**Measured first** (landing 121's compiler, seventeen probes): a bare call in `main` and in an ordinary function, a `fixed` local's initialiser, the name as a value, a module-qualified call, a `requires` clause's call and a `comptime` method in an impl each pass the checker and die in `llc`; `(raw c_f(3i32)) + 1i32` runs; `comptime(…)`, a module binding's initialiser, an array size, a nested comptime body and an `assert_static` proposition run at both legs; a comptime body calling an ordinary function is TYPE-004 (the evaluator's `fold_call` folds a named `comptime func:` alone); `comptime` on a struct, an enum, a module binding, an impl or a trait signature, and `async`/`thread`/`inline`/`noinline` on a struct, parse and mean nothing (DEF-261).

**The rule.** `NITPICK-TYPE-098` (`TYPE_COMPTIME_ONLY`), once, at the function's name. `comptime_fn_admitted(e)` is `fn_comptime` or the new `const_site` -- set around a module binding's initialiser (check_decl's global arm) and around the whole `assert_static` arm (`check_assert_static`, factored out: the arm types its proposition twice); `comptime(…)`'s operand and an array size are typed by nobody (DEF-205) and never ask. `refuse_comptime_fn_use` is reported by `type_ident`'s function arm (the callee of a bare call and the name as a value pass through it; one sentence: write `comptime(NAME(…))` or an ordinary `func:`) and by `type_direct_call` for the module-qualified path (guarded by `callee != 0`: the bare path's callee was typed 0 with its report, D-240). A `comptime` METHOD -- an impl's, a trait's default, a trait's signature -- is refused at its declaration in `check_function` (`self_type != 0`): the evaluator folds a named call and never a method call. The emitter keeps two belts (`emit_direct_call`, `emit_decl_value`): `iv_refused(TYPE-098)` for a road the checker did not ask.

**DEF-262, found by the landing's own program test and fixed.** `check_function` sets the typer's per-function context and nothing reset it for a declaration that is not a function, so a module binding declared after a `pure` function had its initialiser typed under that function's `pure`: `fixed int32:K = raw dbl(21i32);` after `func:sq = … pure never fails` was TYPE-061 where the same text before `sq` compiled -- an order-dependent false refusal on every compiler since 1.5.1, measured on landing 121's compiler (`cm_stale_after`/`cm_stale_before`). `module_level_context` restores the constructor's state at every non-function declaration; the recursion analysis reads a recorded call's caller 0 as none.

**The tests.** `tests/types/rejection/comptime_only.npk` (thirteen sites, one report each; eight controls; the header at a fixed point), `tests/backend/programs/comptime_only.npk` (the roads that remain, at both legs; landing 121's compiler refuses it under DEF-262), `tests/frontend/type_stmt.npk`'s walk sets `fn_comptime` as the checker's does.

**The measurements** (base = landing 121's binaries). THE SWEEP under landing 121's checker and this one over 5,171 files (the tree's units and every .npk of the library and application repositories): 13 sites appeared (the thirteen TYPE-098 of `tests/types/rejection/comptime_only.npk` alone -- three declarations (a `comptime` method in an impl, a `comptime` trait signature and a `comptime` trait default: 50:5, 53:5, 54:5) and ten uses (a bare call in a run-time body 61:19, a contract's `raw sq(n)` 65:51, a `fixed` LOCAL's initialiser 75:25, the `+` node the emitter used to fold 76:20, the name as a function value 77:39, a module-qualified `inner.c_f(3i32)` 78:24, a call as an argument 79:29, a generic comptime function's turbofish call 80:19, a `discard` statement 81:17, a `prove` proposition 82:16); no file of the libraries, the applications or the fuzz corpus gains a site), 10 vanished (the base's eight REACH-002 at the rejection file's `failsafe` (86:5) -- the base accepts the thirteen uses, runs its reach analysis and asks the `failsafe` for the eight identities it does not name (`Unreachable`, `HeapOom`, `HeapBadRequest`, `WildLeak`, `StackExhausted`, `MachineFault`, `IntOverflow`, `OutOfBounds`), which this compiler's refusals precede; and the base's two TYPE-061 at the program test's module bindings (13:24 `raw dbl(21i32)`, 14:31 `raw dbl_plus(3i32)`, each callee 'not declared `pure`'): DEF-262's false refusal, the `pure` flag of the `sq` declared before them) -- 4,234 -> 4,237; per code NITPICK-REACH-002 53 -> 45; NITPICK-TYPE-061 31 -> 29; NITPICK-TYPE-098 0 -> 13. THE EMISSION COMPARISON: 577 programs under both compilers, 576 byte-identical, 0 different, 0 newly refused, 1 refused by the base alone (the new program test `comptime_only.npk`, which the base refuses under DEF-262 -- TYPE-061 at its two module bindings' initialisers, `raw dbl(21i32)` and `raw dbl_plus(3i32)`, typed under the `pure` flag of the `sq` declared before them). The libraries and applications: 4,033 programs, 1,876 byte-identical, 2,157 refused by both, 0 different, 0 newly refused (none; the four fuzz programs landing 121 refused anew -- `bi0077`, `bi0417b`, `me0133`, `ty1754` -- are refused by both compilers now, the base being landing 121's, so the refused-by-both count is 2,153 -> 2,157 and the byte-identical count stands at 1,876). THE RENAMED-COPY LEG: the renamed-copy leg (measured at every landing since 116) RETIRES: nitpick-regex's own tree at 084187a carries the `stack` → `frames` rename since dfa4327 (2026-10-16 11:32), so the main sweep and emission read it -- its 166 programs 133 byte-identical and 33 refused by both, the copy's figures its own now. D-332's count over the five rejection suites: 0 mismatched files. The program suite by hand: 393 programs at both legs, 384 at their expected exit, the nine `// argv:` fixture programs 7 without the harness's driver, by design. The verify suite: 133 files PASS; the problem text of 133 of 133 files byte-identical under both compilers. THE MANIFEST: `npkg verify --record` at this tree -- 7,121 obligations (3,447 discharged, 2,753 open, 0 budget, 916 unencoded, 5 checker), matching, the verified compiler rebuilding itself byte-identically, the floor's 388 obligation(s): 381 discharged, 0 open, 7 budget, 0 unencoded, 0 checker; ### D-356 — LANDED 2026-10-16 (`nitpick-compiler_39`; worktree `wt/39d`, branch `39-d356` on landing 122's commit; landing 123; the user's ruling of 2026-10-15 at 07:50:47 through `management_0`, S-127's recommendation as put: "yes, pass those to compiler_37 too")

**Where it started.** D-338 (landing 90, `nitpick-compiler_28`) gave the compile-time evaluator a payload-less enum variant as a VALUE -- `CV_ENUM`, the enum's type and the variant's position -- to compare with `==`/`!=` and to select on with `pick` inside a `comptime func:` call, and said "nothing else is added": the value did not leave the evaluator. `fixed Ordering:O = Ordering.Less;` stayed NITPICK-TYPE-035, as it had been before the evaluator could hold the value, and `comptime(Ordering.Less)` became NITPICK-TYPE-004 with its own sentence; landing 90 registered the question as S-127 and foreclosed neither answer (one test each). The user settled it on 2026-10-15 at 07:50:47 as D-356, the recommendation as put: admit it in both positions, the constant the tag the variant already has.

**Measured first** on landing 122's compiler, nine probes (`wt/39d/.internal/probes/ec_p1..p9`): a bare variant, a struct's member, an array's member, a payload-carrying enum's payload-less variant, an enum with declared tags, a generic enum's variant and a constant handed to a `pick` are each TYPE-035 at the module binding; `comptime(…)` of a bare variant, of a comptime function's result and of a declared-tag variant are each TYPE-004 with the evaluator's own sentence -- the two tests the decision names, and nothing else refuses the shapes.

**The rule.** the module-constant gate (`const_init_verdict`, type_stmt.npk) no longer answers "not a constant" for a folded enum value and TYPE-035's sentence names "a payload-less variant of a non-generic enum (D-356)"; the forcing form (`type_comptime`, type_expr.npk) no longer refuses one, so the node's type is the enum; and the one scalar renderer (`const_scalar_text`, ir_expr.npk) gains an enum arm, `const_enum_text`: the evaluator's value is the variant's POSITION (`cv_enum`, D-338), the constant is its TAG through `variant_tag_of` -- the declared literal where one is given, the position otherwise, D-342's one rule, which the constructor, the `pick` and the drop body read too -- in the shape `ll_enum_tag_const` (ir_types.npk, beside `ll_enum_body`) writes: the `i32` for a payload-less enum, `{ i32 TAG, [N x iK] zeroinitializer }` for a tagged one, the value `emit_ctor` stores for the same variant (zero first, then the tag); "" where the position names no variant, the frontend's broken promise and never a number. ### D-359 — LANDED 2026-10-17 (`nitpick-compiler_39`; worktree `wt/39e`, branch `39-d359` on landing 123's commit; landing 124; the user's ruling of 2026-10-15 at 07:54:31 through `management_0`: "go with the S-37 recommendation"; planned first at `.internal/handoff_35/D359_PLAN.md`)

**Where it started.** 1.5.2b's planning (2026-09-05) measured `impl:bool:Ord` admitted -- a pair the prelude does not cover -- and asked whether an orphan rule should close it, since two libraries writing it would collide under coherence in the program importing both; the user left it for the library era (S-37) with the shape "an impl names at least one type or trait declared in its own manifest's tree", and settled it as D-359 on 2026-10-15.

**Measured first.** MEASURED FIRST, textually, over the three trees (`scratchpad/orphan_census.py`: every `impl:` whose target names no type declared in its repository and whose trait is not declared there): the compiler's tree 703 impls and one candidate, `tests/grammar/whole_grammar.npk`'s parse-only blanket sample whose `Loggable` is declared nowhere; nitpick-regex 5 impls, 0; nitpick-time 8 impls, 1 -- `tests/probe/probe19_pod_owner_refused.npk`'s `impl:string:Copy`, which expects TYPE-087 and is TYPE-099 under the one-report rule; nitpick-parse, nitpick-sockets, nitpick-tui and the application 0 impls; the fuzz corpus 136 impls, 3 -- `tr0236` (`impl:int32:Ord`, `expect: refuse:NITPICK-TYPE-013`: the code moves to TYPE-099), `tr0237` (`impl:bool:Ord`, `expect: compile`: OVERTURNED by D-359), `tr0347` (an inherent blanket, refused before by the blanket-names-a-trait rule, which precedes: unchanged).

**The rule.** `NITPICK-TYPE-099` (`TYPE_ORPHAN_IMPL`, type_codes.npk) at the impl, once, asked in `collect_one_impl` (type_trait.npk) after the target and the trait resolve (a refused one has had its sentence, D-240). A file's TREE is the directory of the nearest `nitpick.toml` above it (`srcmgr_tree_of`, source.npk: the walk `front_set_root` makes for the main file, D-236, made once per file and kept in `SourceFile.tree`), else the program's root -- a manifest-less project is one tree -- and "<synthetic>" for a file nothing opened, so the prelude is its own tree and a derive's generated impl, which names its subject by construction, is never asked. The impl names a type of its tree when the target's declaration, or ANY named type among its type arguments or element types (`type_names_tree`, the shape of `type_mentions_params_of`), is declared in a file of that tree -- `impl:List<Pt>:ToString` names `Pt`; a generic parameter and a builtin name nothing -- and a trait of its tree when the trait segment's declaration is; an inherent impl's target must be the tree's own, a blanket impl's trait must be. The refused entry STAYS in the impl table, marked `orphan`, so a call through it finds its method and reports nothing more (1.6.1c's lesson), and `check_coherence` and `check_impls_complete` skip it: `impl:int32:Ord` is TYPE-099 where it was TYPE-013 against the prelude's row, `impl:string:Copy` TYPE-099 where it was TYPE-087. The impl table reaches the source manager through a `wild` handle the driver sets before collection (`impltable_set_sources`, as the emitter's context holds its tables -- a plain parameter stored there was BORROW-011), NULL in a unit's fixture, which asks no question: one file is one tree. The sentence names each side -- "`bool`, a builtin and `Ord`, the prelude's" -- and the two spellings that stand: your own trait for the type, or the trait for a type of your own.
