### DEF-242 — LANDED 2026-10-17 (`nitpick-compiler_39`; worktree `wt/39g`, branch `39-def242` on landing 125's commit; landing 126; registered at landing 97; first after 125 by the author's reorder of 2026-10-16 at 23:35:51 -- Q37, through `management_1`: "nice! for q37, yes i am fine with that.")

**Where it started.** Landing 97's emission comparison (DEF-231, the frac section) showed 561 of 561 tree programs and 2,435 of 2,435 library programs different in text by the site table's renumbering alone, and `nitpick-compiler_32` registered DEF-242 reading it: D-179's error-origin site table (`@npk.sitep.N`, `@npk.site.lines`, `@npk.site.paths`; read by `site_path`/`site_line` when a `failsafe` names a trap's origin) numbered the prelude's sites first and the program's after them, and every emission carried every prelude site -- the tables were written BEFORE D-262's trim, so they were roots the trim kept whole: some 290 `prelude.npk` rows and a 300-entry pair of tables in a program that calls none of those bodies -- so a prelude edit moved EVERY program's text (landing 97: 561 of 561 tree programs and 2,435 of 2,435 library programs by the renumbering alone), the library side's per-program digests moved across every such re-pin (F39), and an emission comparison had to be canonicalised (`meta/roadmap/1.6/tools/sitenorm.py`) before it said anything. The plan is `.internal/handoff_35/D242_PLAN.md`.

**What the code did.** `irw_site(w, path, line)` (ir_writer.npk) is the one registration -- a (path, line) row deduplicated in first-sight order, the id `i + 1` (0 the runtime's reserved site) -- and every consumer wrote the id inline through `irw_line` as one of two call shapes; `emit_all` wrote the tables (`emit_site_tables`) BEFORE it trimmed the items, so the tables were roots the trim kept whole, every site ever registered in every program.

**The change.** `emit_all` (emit_program.npk) trims the items FIRST (`irw_trim_items`), then `irw_renumber_sites` (ir_writer.npk, new) scans the kept text for the two call shapes `call void @npk_chain_reset(i32 N)` / `call void @npk_chain_push(i32 N)` -- the whole surface of a site reference: every writer goes through `irw_line` (35 sites: ir_expr 24, ir_stmt 6, ir_rules 2, emit_program 2, ir_func 1), and a writer nobody anticipated is caught by the belt, never misnumbered in silence -- maps the referenced ids to dense new ids from 1 in ascending OLD id order (registration order, D-078: deterministic), rewrites them in the text and keeps the old ids in `IrWriter.site_keep`; `emit_site_tables` then writes `@npk.sitep.K`, `@npk.site.lines` and `@npk.site.paths` for the kept sites alone, each row's path and line read through its old id, the two lookups' bound the kept count + 1. The ids are the emission's own constants, so nothing changes at run time: a trap's origin prints the same path and line, the chain's semantics stand (reset at an origin, push at a relay), the runtime's reserved site 0 stays, no obligation row, guard or node id moves, the explorer's step counts are untouched (the tables are data). THE BELT, in both runners: `check_site_table_dense` (harness.py) and `ir_site_table_dense` (npkg/irscan.npk), asked of every module the compiler under test emits beside the prelude-trim belt (a program's emission, the verified build's, the compiler's own; never the snapshot's, the 1.5.2d rule) -- the set of ids the two call shapes reference equals {1..n} where n+1 is the lines table's length (row 0 the runtime's reserved site); a spare row is the renumbering not running, an id past the table a writer that spells a chain call another way; a module with no table and no call passes (the floor, the shim); each runner's self-check plants three cases (a dense table, a spare row, a call past the table), the harness's texts byte for byte.

**Found on the way.** FOUND BY THE LANDING'S OWN PROGRAM TEST AND FIXED WITH IT, DEF-263: the `npk_site_path` lookup `emit_site_tables` writes answered `{ ptr, len, len }` -- the string's third word, its `cap`, was the LENGTH, which is the ownership bit set (D-183: `cap == 0` is a body the value does not own) -- so `string:origin = site_path(chain_site(0i32));` in a `failsafe` freed the `@npk.sitep.N` constant at the scope's exit: `npk.drop` -> `npk_dalloc` -> `npk_heap_bad` -> `HeapBadRequest` inside `failsafe`, the re-entry exit 70 at both legs, on every compiler since D-179 landed the table (1.1.6). `origin_chain.npk`, the one program of the tree reading the chain, renders ids through `site_line` alone, and the three fuzz programs that call the lookups (`bi0263`, `bi0264`, `bi0265`) read site 0, whose answer is `zeroinitializer` (a view already), so the fault had no witness until the new test bound a real site's answer. The lookup writes `i64 0` as the third word now -- the answer is a VIEW of the table's constant, never dropped and never freed -- and BUILTIN_REFERENCE's row says so.

**Measured.** MEASURED FIRST on landing 123's compiler (wt/39g's first base; the checker is untouched, so the sweep cannot move): a floor-only probe (`wt/39g/.internal/probes/st_floor.npk`, thirteen `?!` sites of its own) emitted `@npk.site.lines = internal constant [307 x i32]`, 306 `@npk.sitep.` rows (293 of them `prelude.npk`) and thirteen chain calls (ids 294..306) in 59,224 bytes; under this compiler the same probe carries 14 rows, thirteen `@npk.sitep.` rows (its own file's) and the same thirteen calls (ids 1..13) in 25,864 bytes; the compiler's own emission 4,357 -> 4,092 rows (31,926,761 -> 31,895,403 bytes). Every `npk_chain_` line of both compilers' emissions that is not a `declare` and not one of the two call shapes is one of the compiler's own string constants (its emitter's text, `c"call void @npk_chain_"`), so the two shapes ARE the whole surface; the belt over the four emissions: the new two dense, the base's with 293 and 265 spare rows. `origin_chain.npk` (the tree's one reader of the chain, pinning two of its own lines) exits 42 at both legs under the new compiler; the whole program suite at both legs shows the nine `// argv:` fixture programs and nothing else.

**Tests.** `tests/backend/site_table.npk` (a writer-level unit: three registered sites, a text referencing the first and the third -- an origin and a relay -- and nothing referencing the second: the second is dropped, the third becomes 2, `site_keep` reads [1, 3], a `declare` without a number and a string constant spelling the call are untouched), `tests/backend/programs/site_table_rows.npk` (a prelude raise -- `list_pop` on an empty list -- reaches `failsafe`, which reads the origin back through `site_path`/`site_line` and walks the whole table: the kept prelude bodies' rows (`list_init<int32>` and `list_pop<int32>`: four at this writing), the file's own, nothing else, under forty rows where every compiler before 126 wrote some three hundred; exit 42 at both legs; landing 125's compiler exits 70 at both legs -- DEF-263's fault inside `failsafe`), and each runner's self-check's three planted cases (`site-table-dense-control`, `site-table-spare-row`, `site-table-past-end`).
